4. Những hạn chế thực tế của Tauri

Đây là điều mình muốn bạn đặc biệt chú ý.

    App chạy foreground: Chat và call có thể hoạt động tốt nếu triển khai WebRTC đúng cách.

    App xuống background: Android có thể hạn chế hoặc tạm dừng WebView, khiến cuộc gọi bị gián đoạn. Tauri có tài liệu về giới hạn background throttling. 
    Tauri
    +1

    Nhận cuộc gọi khi app đóng: Cần Push Notification (APNs/FCM), native integration và cơ chế khởi động cuộc gọi.

    Call khi khóa màn hình: Cần tích hợp native audio session, background execution và quản lý vòng đời cuộc gọi.

    Cuộc gọi đến như WhatsApp: Nên tích hợp native CallKit trên iOS và ConnectionService trên Android.

Tauri hỗ trợ viết plugin native bằng Swift và Kotlin để xử lý các chức năng này.

Được\! Và mình nghĩ có thể làm một dự án khá hay: Mini Zalo – ứng dụng nhắn tin riêng tư, tự chọn nơi lưu dữ liệu, chạy đa nền tảng.

Mục tiêu là một ứng dụng kiểu Zalo/WhatsApp thu nhỏ, nhưng có điểm khác biệt: người dùng tự quyết định dữ liệu nằm ở đâu. Không bắt buộc tất cả phải lưu trên server của bạn.

Mình đề xuất thiết kế theo kiểu Local-first + Multi-storage + Self-hosted.

## 1\. Mini Zalo sẽ chạy trên những thiết bị nào?

Android

Hỗ trợ

iPhone / iOS

Hỗ trợ

Windows

Hỗ trợ

Linux

Hỗ trợ

Web Browser

Hỗ trợ

Tablet

Hỗ trợ

## 2\. Người dùng tự chọn nơi lưu dữ liệu

Đây là phần mình thích nhất trong ý tưởng của bạn.

1\. Lưu trực tiếp trên điện thoại / máy tính

Local

- Tin nhắn và danh bạ lưu trong SQLite.
- File ảnh/video lưu trên máy.
- Không cần thuê server.
- Có thể dùng offline.
- Nếu mất máy mà chưa backup thì có nguy cơ mất dữ liệu.

2\. Google Drive

Cloud backup

- Backup tin nhắn, hình ảnh, file.
- Khôi phục khi đổi điện thoại.
- Đồng bộ dữ liệu giữa các thiết bị qua cơ chế riêng.

Lưu ý: Google Drive nên dùng để backup/restore, không nên dùng như database chat trực tiếp vì dễ phát sinh xung đột khi nhiều thiết bị cùng ghi dữ liệu.

3\. NAS / ổ cứng mạng

Private storage

- Synology, QNAP hoặc NAS tự dựng.
- Lưu ảnh, video, tài liệu.
- Có thể chạy server riêng bằng Docker.
- Truy cập từ điện thoại và máy tính khi ở ngoài nhà.

4\. Server riêng – Big Boss

Enterprise

- Mỗi người có tài khoản riêng.
- Đồng bộ tin nhắn theo thời gian thực.
- Quản lý nhóm, phân quyền, nhiều thiết bị.
- Mở rộng hàng nghìn hoặc nhiều người dùng hơn tùy cấu hình.
- Tự quản lý dữ liệu và hạ tầng.

Điểm quan trọng: Không cần chọn một nơi lưu duy nhất cho toàn bộ ứng dụng. Có thể lưu tin nhắn trong database, file trên NAS, backup lên Google Drive và đồng bộ qua server. Người dùng được chọn cấu hình phù hợp.

## 3\. Nên viết bằng phần mềm gì?

Mình đề xuất bộ công nghệ này:

| Thành phần | Công nghệ | Vai trò |
| --- | --- | --- |
| App | Flutter / Dart | Android, iOS, Windows, Linux |
| Database local | SQLite / Drift | Lưu tin nhắn offline |
| Backend | Supabase | Tài khoản, dữ liệu, đồng bộ |
| Database server | PostgreSQL | Lưu dữ liệu chính |
| Realtime | WebSocket | Tin nhắn tức thời |
| File storage | S3 / MinIO | Ảnh, video, file |
| Push notification | FCM / APNs | Thông báo |
| Coding AI | OpenCode | Hỗ trợ viết code |

Flutter có hỗ trợ chính thức Android, iOS, Windows, Linux và Web.

 Chốt cấu hình và lập dự án

Một lời khuyên: Hãy làm một codebase Flutter duy nhất, thiết kế storage adapter ngay từ đầu. Sau này người dùng cá nhân dùng miễn phí, người có NAS tự lưu, còn Big Boss có thể thuê hoặc tự vận hành server riêng. iOS cần thêm máy Mac/Xcode để build và ký ứng dụng iPhone.

Mục tiêu là một Mini Zalo mà bạn sở hữu mã nguồn và có thể tự triển khai, không bị khóa vào một nhà cung cấp cloud nào.


Đúng rồi. Mình sẽ thiết kế Mini Zalo theo nguyên tắc người dùng toàn quyền chọn nơi lưu dữ liệu và nơi backup dữ liệu, hai chức năng này độc lập hoàn toàn với nhau.

Ví dụ: lưu tin nhắn trên điện thoại nhưng backup lên NAS, hoặc lưu trên server nhưng backup sang Google Drive.

## 1. Hai hệ thống độc lập

## A. Nơi lưu dữ liệu chính

StorageNgười dùng được chọn một hoặc nhiều nơi lưu tùy cấu hình.

Ổ điện thoại / máy tính

Local

Google Drive

NAS / Synology / QNAP

Đường dẫn ổ cứng / thư mục mạng

Server riêng / Big Boss

## B. Nơi backup dữ liệu

BackupCho phép chọn một hoặc nhiều nơi khác với nơi lưu chính.

- Điện thoại / máy tính.
- Google Drive.
- NAS.
- Ổ cứng ngoài / thư mục mạng.
- Server riêng.

## 2. Ví dụ cấu hình thực tế

| Người dùng | Lưu chính | Backup |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Cá nhân | Điện thoại | Google Drive |
| Gia đình | NAS | Google Drive |
| Người dùng nâng cao | Ổ cứng riêng | NAS |
| Doanh nghiệp | Server | NAS + Server dự phòng |
| Big Boss | Server riêng | Server khác + ổ cứng |

## 3. Những chức năng cần có

- Tự động backup theo giờ, ngày hoặc tuần.
- Backup khi có Wi-Fi.
- Backup chỉ khi đang sạc.
- Backup thủ công.
- Khôi phục dữ liệu trên thiết bị mới.
- Kiểm tra dung lượng và trạng thái backup.
- Mã hóa bản backup bằng mật khẩu hoặc khóa riêng.
- Kiểm tra tính toàn vẹn dữ liệu.
- Chuyển nơi lưu mà không mất lịch sử chat.
- Xuất dữ liệu để không bị phụ thuộc vào ứng dụng.

Một điểm kỹ thuật quan trọng: Đường link ổ cứng cần hỗ trợ đúng giao thức như SMB, WebDAV, SFTP hoặc S3. Không phải URL nào cũng có thể dùng làm ổ lưu trữ. Trên iOS và Android, quyền truy cập file cũng có giới hạn riêng.

## 5. Mình muốn bổ sung một nguyên tắc rất quan trọng

Không khóa dữ liệu vào Mini Zalo.

Người dùng có thể:

- Đổi điện thoại nhưng giữ nguyên lịch sử.
- Chuyển từ Google Drive sang NAS.
- Chuyển từ NAS lên server riêng.
- Xuất toàn bộ tin nhắn, hình ảnh, file.
- Rời ứng dụng nhưng vẫn giữ dữ liệu của mình.

Mình sẽ đưa nguyên tắc này vào kiến trúc kỹ thuật ngay từ đầu.

Đề xuất thêm: Cho phép chọn nhiều nơi lưu chính và thiết lập ưu tiên, ví dụ Local là chính, NAS là bản đồng bộ, Google Drive là bản backup. Khi một nơi mất kết nối, ứng dụng vẫn hoạt động và đồng bộ lại khi kết nối phục hồi.


Nếu viết riêng Kotlin cho Android và Swift cho iOS, mình đánh giá đây là phương án rất tốt cho Mini Zalo, đặc biệt vì dự án của bạn có yêu cầu lưu trữ linh hoạt: điện thoại, Google Drive, NAS, ổ cứng mạng và server riêng.

Thay vì cố gắng dùng một codebase cho tất cả, chúng ta xây dựng hai ứng dụng native riêng, nhưng dùng chung backend, giao thức, database schema và quy tắc đồng bộ.

Mình gọi kiến trúc này là Native Multi-Platform + Shared Backend.

# 3. Hệ thống lưu trữ đa lựa chọn

Đây là phần quan trọng nhất của Mini Zalo.

Mình sẽ thiết kế Storage Adapter để Android và iOS có cùng một giao thức lưu trữ, nhưng mỗi nền tảng có implementation riêng.

## Storage Manager

Storage Interface

Giao diện thống nhất cho mọi loại lưu trữ

Local Storage

SQLite, file nội bộ

Google Drive

Cloud API

NAS

SMB, WebDAV, SFTP

Private Server

S3, HTTPS API

Ví dụ cấu hình:

| Dữ liệu chính | Backup |
| --- | --- |
| Android | Google Drive |
| iPhone | NAS |
| Windows | Ổ cứng ngoài |
| Server | NAS thứ hai |
| NAS | Google Drive |

Một số giới hạn cần xử lý:

- iOS không cho ứng dụng tự do truy cập toàn bộ ổ cứng; phải thông qua Files và quyền được cấp.
- Android dùng Storage Access Framework để người dùng cấp quyền thư mục.
- NAS cần giao thức mạng thích hợp; SMB không được hỗ trợ trực tiếp giống nhau trên mọi nền tảng.
- Google Drive cần OAuth và quản lý token an toàn.
- Backup phải có versioning, kiểm tra checksum và cơ chế khôi phục.

# 4. Backend dùng chung cho cả hai app

Mình đề xuất Node.js + TypeScript cho backend.

| Service | Nhiệm vụ |
| --- | --- |
| Auth | Đăng nhập, đăng ký, phiên đăng nhập |
| User | Hồ sơ, avatar, danh bạ |
| Chat | Tin nhắn riêng và nhóm |
| Realtime | WebSocket |
| Media | Ảnh, video, file |
| Storage | Quản lý các nơi lưu |
| Backup | Lập lịch, backup, restore |
| Sync | Đồng bộ nhiều thiết bị |
| Notification | Push notification |

Database:

- PostgreSQL: dữ liệu tài khoản, nhóm, metadata.
- SQLite: dữ liệu local từng thiết bị.
- Redis: cache, presence và hàng đợi khi cần.
- S3/MinIO: lưu file và media.

Với E2EE, nội dung tin nhắn được mã hóa ở thiết bị trước khi gửi; server chỉ lưu ciphertext và metadata tối thiểu cần thiết.

# 6. Còn Windows và Linux thì sao?

Vì bạn muốn ứng dụng chạy cả Windows và Linux, mình đề xuất có thêm desktop client sau:

- Windows: Kotlin Multiplatform Desktop hoặc Tauri.
- Linux: Tauri hoặc Kotlin Multiplatform Desktop.
- Web: React nếu cần truy cập qua trình duyệt.

Tất cả dùng chung API và protocol với Android/iOS.

Không cần phát triển cả năm nền tảng cùng một lúc.

# 7. Lộ trình phát triển mình đề xuất

1. Giai đoạn 1 – Core Platform
   Thiết kế database, API, authentication, storage adapter và giao thức đồng bộ.
2. Giai đoạn 2 – Android Kotlin
   Chat 1-1, nhóm, gửi file, local storage và backup.
3. Giai đoạn 3 – iOS Swift
   Xây dựng app native tương thích API, đồng bộ và E2EE.
4. Giai đoạn 4 – Desktop
   Windows, Linux, quản lý file và NAS.
5. Giai đoạn 5 – Big Boss
   Self-hosted server, multi-tenant, quản trị, mở rộng và monitoring.

# 8\. Có một quyết định kiến trúc cần chốt

Theo mình có hai kiểu sản phẩm khác nhau:

A. Mini Zalo độc lập (khuyên dùng)

- Người dùng tự tạo tài khoản Mini Zalo.
- Có server mặc định nhưng không bắt buộc dùng server của bạn.
- Có thể tự host server riêng.
- Có thể sử dụng hoàn toàn local cho những tính năng phù hợp.
- Người dùng chọn nơi lưu và backup độc lập.

B. Mini Zalo P2P / Decentralized

- Các thiết bị kết nối trực tiếp khi có thể.
- Không phụ thuộc một server trung tâm cho mọi hoạt động.
- NAS hoặc máy tính cá nhân có thể làm node.
- Phức tạp hơn về NAT traversal, offline delivery, đồng bộ và quản lý khóa.

1\. Kiến trúc hệ thống

hybrid A. Hybrid – Server + Local + NAS + Cloud

B. P2P – Ưu tiên kết nối trực tiếp

C. Cả hai – hỗ trợ cả Hybrid và P2P

2\. Windows / Linux

tauri Tauri + React

Kotlin Multiplatform

Làm sau, ưu tiên Android + iOS

3\. Tài khoản

both Đăng ký tài khoản hoặc dùng local không cần tài khoản

Bắt buộc có tài khoản

# 2. Gradle là gì?

Bạn hỏi rất đúng. Mình giải thích đơn giản nhé.

Hãy tưởng tượng bạn đang xây một căn nhà:

| Công cụ | Vai trò |
| --- | --- |
| Android Studio | Xưởng làm việc |
| Kotlin | Ngôn ngữ xây dựng |
| Gradle | Quản lý quá trình xây dựng |
| Android SDK | Bộ công cụ Android |
| OpenCode | Trợ lý AI viết code |
| Room | Quản lý database |
| APK | Sản phẩm cài đặt lên điện thoại |

Gradle không phải ngôn ngữ lập trình.

Gradle là hệ thống Build Automation, chịu trách nhiệm:

- Tải và quản lý thư viện cần thiết.
- Biên dịch Kotlin.
- Xây dựng các module.
- Chạy kiểm thử.
- Đóng gói APK/AAB.
- Quản lý cấu hình build.

Ví dụ khi chạy:

powershell

```powershell
.\gradlew assembleDebug
```

Gradle sẽ xây dựng ứng dụng Android và tạo file APK debug.

# 3. Kiến trúc KONNEX

Mình đề xuất cấu trúc Multi-module ngay từ đầu.

KONNEX ANDROID APP

Kotlin + Jetpack Compose

Database và repository

Business logic

storage

Lưu trữ dữ liệu

security

Mã hóa và khóa

Đồng bộ thiết bị

backup

Backup và restore

network

API và WebSocket

calling

Voice / Video call

ANDROID PLATFORM

Android OS · Native APIs · Telecom · FCM

| Giai đoạn | Nội dung |

| 1 | Project foundation, Gradle, kiến trúc |

| 3 | Local database, tài khoản |
| 4 | Chat 1-1, chat nhóm, file |
| 5 | Storage Anywhere |
| 6 | Backup & Restore |
| 7 | Sync Engine, nhiều thiết bị |
| 8 | E2EE, bảo mật |
| 9 | Voice/video call, notification, background |

Lưu ý: chúng ta thiết kế các module bảo mật, đồng bộ và cuộc gọi từ đầu, nhưng không cần triển khai toàn bộ ngay ở giai đoạn 1.

# 5. Quy tắc quan trọng về ổ D và ổ C

Mình muốn cấu hình:

| Thành phần | Vị trí |
| --- | --- |
| Source code | D |
| Gradle cache | D |
| Gradle wrapper | Trong project D |
| Android SDK | D nếu hiện tại đã cài hoặc có thể chuyển an toàn |
| JDK | Ưu tiên D |
| Build output | D |
| APK | D |
| Tài liệu | D |

Không tự ý di chuyển Android SDK hoặc JDK hiện có trước khi kiểm tra cấu hình Android Studio.

# 6. Bây giờ kiểm tra máy trước khi tạo project

Vì bạn đã cài Android Studio, OpenCode/FreeCode và Gradle rồi, chúng ta không cần tải lại hoặc cài lại một cách mù quáng.

Mở PowerShell và chạy lần lượt đoạn sau. Đây chỉ là lệnh kiểm tra, không xóa hay sửa dữ liệu.

powershell

```powershell

Write-Host "===== KONNEX ENVIRONMENT CHECK =====" -ForegroundColor Cyan

Write-Host "`n[1] Java"
java -version

Write-Host "`n[2] Gradle"
gradle -v

Write-Host "`n[3] Android SDK"
Write-Host "ANDROID_HOME=$env:ANDROID_HOME"
Write-Host "ANDROID_SDK_ROOT=$env:ANDROID_SDK_ROOT"

Write-Host "`n[4] Kotlin"
if (Get-Command kotlinc -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue) {
    kotlinc -version
} else {
    Write-Host "Kotlin CLI not found (may be managed by Gradle)"
}

Write-Host "`n[5] OpenCode"
if (Get-Command opencode -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue) {
    opencode --version
} else {
    Write-Host "OpenCode command not found"
}

Write-Host "`n[6] FreeCode"
if (Get-Command freecode -ErrorAction SilentlyContinue) {
    freecode --version
} else {
    Write-Host "FreeCode command not found"
}

Write-Host "`n[7] Disk space"
Get-PSDrive -PSProvider FileSystem |
    Select-Object Name,
    @{Name="FreeGB";Expression={[math]::Round($_.Free/1GB,2)}},
    @{Name="UsedGB";Expression={[math]::Round($_.Used/1GB,2)}}

Write-Host "`n[8] Project folder"
Test-Path "D:\AI\appDevelopment\7miniChatCall"
```

Sau khi chạy, gửi mình kết quả PowerShell.

Mình sẽ dựa vào đúng môi trường máy bạn để viết cấu hình Gradle, Android SDK và OpenCode. Không bắt bạn cài lại những gì đã có.

Mục tiêu bước tiếp theo: tạo được project KONNEX đầu tiên trên ổ D, mở thành công bằng Android Studio và build được APK debug. Sau đó mới giao từng module cho OpenCode thực hiện. :))

## 1. Prompt khởi tạo KONNEX cho OpenCode

Mở PowerShell:

powershell

markdown

```markdown

# PROJECT: KONNEX - ANDROID NATIVE FOUNDATION

You are the lead Android architect and senior Kotlin engineer.

Your task is to BUILD the initial foundation of KONNEX, a completely original private messaging application.

IMPORTANT:
- Do actual work in the current workspace.
- Do not merely explain or generate example snippets.
- Inspect the existing environment before changing anything.
- Create files, configure Gradle, implement modules, build and test the application.
- Work autonomously where safe.
- Do not ask the user to manually perform routine development steps.

==================================================
1. PROJECT LOCATION
==================================================

Project root:
D:\AI\appDevelopment\7miniChatCall

Platform:
Android first.

Language:
Kotlin.

UI:
Jetpack Compose.

IDE:
Android Studio.

AI coding:
OpenCode.

Gradle Wrapper.

Temurin JDK 17.

D:\AI\Android\Sdk

- Java 17.0.21
- Gradle 8.9
- Kotlin CLI not installed (Gradle plugin may provide Kotlin)
- Android SDK at D:\AI\Android\Sdk
- OpenCode 1.18.31
- Windows 11

Disk:
- C: approximately 76 GB free
- D: approximately 1111 GB free

IMPORTANT DISK RULE:
Use D: for project files, Gradle caches, build outputs,
downloaded dependencies where configurable, and SDK tools.

Do not unnecessarily download or reinstall existing tools.

Do not move existing SDK/JDK installations without permission.

Never delete existing user files.

==================================================
2. PRODUCT IDENTITY
==================================================

Application name: KONNEX

Product description:
Private Messaging Platform.

This must be an original product.

Do not use:
- Zalo name or branding.
- Zalo logos or assets.
- Zalo proprietary source code.
- Zalo UI copied from screenshots.
- Any third-party proprietary branding.

Create an original visual identity for KONNEX.

Suggested design:
Modern, minimal, professional, privacy-focused.

==================================================
3. ARCHITECTURE
==================================================

Build a modular Android application.

Use Clean Architecture principles with
clear separation of concerns.

Proposed modules:

:app

:core:common
:core:model
:core:database
:core:network
:core:security

:backup
:sync
:calling

You may adjust module boundaries when technically justified.
Document every architectural deviation.

Do not create empty modules without a purpose.
Every module must have a clear responsibility.

==================================================

==================================================

Use compatible stable versions of:

- Kotlin
- Android Gradle Plugin
- Gradle Wrapper
- Jetpack Compose
- Compose Material 3
- AndroidX Navigation Compose
- Room
- Kotlin Coroutines
- Flow
- Ktor Client
- Kotlin Serialization
- Hilt or another justified dependency injection solution
- WorkManager
- Android Keystore APIs

Use version catalogs:

gradle/libs.versions.toml

Use convention plugins if appropriate.

Use JDK 17.

Do not blindly use the globally installed Gradle.
Use Gradle Wrapper.

Choose mutually compatible versions.
Document the selected versions.

Do not use alpha or experimental dependencies
unless absolutely necessary.

==================================================
5. STORAGE ARCHITECTURE
==================================================

KONNEX must support user-controlled storage.

Design independent interfaces for:

1. Local device storage
2. Google Drive
3. NAS
4. Network storage
5. Private server

Possible protocols:
- Local filesystem
- Google Drive API
- WebDAV
- SFTP
- SMB through a suitable implementation
- S3-compatible storage
- HTTPS API

Important:

Primary storage and backup storage must be
two independent configurations.

Allow multiple backup destinations.

Do not use Google Drive synchronized folders
as an active SQLite database location.

Do not store encryption keys in plaintext.

Implement interfaces and local storage first.

Do not implement fake Google Drive, NAS or server
connections.

If credentials or API configuration are missing,
create documented integration interfaces and
report what is needed.

==================================================
6. BACKUP AND SYNC DESIGN
==================================================

Design for:

- Multiple devices
- Offline operation
- Retry after connection failure
- Conflict resolution
- Versioned backups
- Checksum verification
- Backup restoration
- Export and import
- Incremental synchronization
- Attachment management

Use a transactional local database.

Do not claim that synchronization is implemented
until it actually works.

Document the synchronization protocol.

==================================================
7. SECURITY DESIGN
==================================================

Prepare architecture for:

- End-to-end encryption
- Android Keystore
- Secure key management
- Secure local storage
- Encrypted backup
- Authentication
- Session management

Do not invent a custom cryptographic algorithm.

Do not claim E2EE is complete until it has been
implemented and independently reviewed.

Clearly distinguish:
- Planned security
- Implemented security
- Tested security

==================================================
8. CALLING ARCHITECTURE
==================================================

Prepare a dedicated calling module.

Future capabilities:
- Voice calls
- Video calls
- WebRTC
- Foreground services
- Android Telecom integration
- Incoming call notifications
- Background call management
- Lock-screen call handling

Do not fake working calls.

Document native Android lifecycle requirements.

==================================================
9. INITIAL APPLICATION
==================================================

Create a working initial KONNEX application.

It must include:

- Original KONNEX branding
- Splash/onboarding screen
- Main navigation
- Chat list screen
- Conversation screen mockup
- Contacts screen
- Profile screen
- Settings screen
- Storage settings screen
- Backup settings screen

Use original UI components.

Separate UI from business logic.

Mock data must be clearly identified as mock data.

Do not pretend real messaging or authentication
works without a backend.

==================================================
10. PROJECT DOCUMENTATION
==================================================

Create:

README.md

==================================================

==================================================

1. Inspect the current workspace first.
2. Never overwrite unknown existing files.
3. Never delete user data.
4. Never run destructive commands without permission.
5. Keep all project source in D:.
6. Prefer Gradle cache on D:.
7. Use reproducible builds.
8. Add unit tests for core logic.
9. Run Gradle build after implementation.
10. Fix compilation errors before finishing.
11. Do not hide build failures.
12. Do not report success unless verified.

Do not download unnecessary tools.

If internet access or a dependency is unavailable,
report it accurately.

==================================================
12. EXECUTION PLAN
==================================================

PHASE 0:
Inspect environment and existing workspace.

PHASE 1:
Create Gradle project foundation.

PHASE 2:
Configure modules and dependency graph.

PHASE 3:
Implement core architecture and database foundation.

PHASE 4:
Implement original Compose design system and
initial KONNEX navigation/screens.

PHASE 5:
Implement local storage interfaces and settings.

PHASE 6:
Create backup and sync interfaces.

PHASE 7:
Prepare security and calling architecture.

PHASE 8:
Documentation, tests and build verification.

Execute these phases in order.

Do not stop after producing a plan.

Work through the phases that can safely be
completed in this environment.

If the task becomes too large for one session,
finish the current safe checkpoint and document
the exact remaining work.

==================================================
13. FINAL REPORT
==================================================

When finished, report:

1. Files created.
2. Modules implemented.
3. Technology versions selected.

BEGIN EXECUTION.
```

## 2. Cách phối hợp từ giờ

## 3. Một lưu ý quan trọng

Đừng yêu cầu OpenCode làm toàn bộ Mini Messenger hoàn chỉnh trong một lần. Prompt trên yêu cầu nó xây nền móng đầy đủ, nhưng vẫn có checkpoint và kiểm thử.

Đặc biệt, những phần như E2EE, Google Drive, NAS, WebRTC và gọi điện nền không được phép báo là hoàn thành nếu mới chỉ tạo interface.

Bước tiếp theo: Cho OpenCode chạy prompt trên. Khi nó trả về báo cáo cuối cùng hoặc gặp lỗi Gradle, gửi nguyên kết quả cho mình. Mình sẽ viết prompt sửa lỗi hoặc prompt triển khai giai đoạn tiếp theo.

Chúng ta sẽ dùng cách này để xây KONNEX: AI làm việc, bạn không phải ngồi viết từng dòng code. :))




I'll start by inspecting the existing workspace and environment before making any changes.

Empty workspace. Let me locate the JDK, Gradle, and SDK component versions.

The combined probe hung (likely a `cmd.exe` call). Let me probe more precisely.

Found JDK 17 at `D:/AI/Java/jdk-17` and a Gradle dir on D:. Checking versions and offline cache state.

Network is reachable (Google Maven + Gradle distributions). Now let me pin exact versions already present in the cache so the build stays fast and reproducible.

Environment is clear: JDK 17 + Gradle 8.9 + SDK 35 all pre-installed on D:, with a warm dependency cache (AGP 8.7.3, Kotlin 2.0.21, Compose BOM 2024.10.01). I'll scaffold the build, then implement modules phase by phase.




Wrapper is in place. Now the version catalog and Gradle convention plugins (build-logic) that keep 34 modules' build files tiny.




Now the foundation modules — pure-JVM core utilities and models.




Fail-fast checkpoint: compile the convention plugins and the first two modules.

Long builds must run in the background here. Starting it with logging and polling.

While that builds, I'll implement the seven domain modules (pure Kotlin interfaces + use cases, framework-free).













The convention jar was built by the interrupted run and is stale. Let me inspect it.

Found the bug: Gradle wrote plugin descriptors from bare class names. Fixing that, then writing the Android core modules.







Now the original KONNEX design system (brand mark drawn in Compose, palette, typography, components).







Fixing the archive compile error and implementing the data layer.







Now the storage providers, backup engine, sync engine and calling module.










While modules compile, I'm adding shared formatting + scheduling contracts, then the feature screens.






















Now the application module: composition root, navigation, launcher icon and manifest.







While Gradle builds, I'll write the required documentation set.




Three compile errors: Android library modules lacked coroutines on the compile classpath, and one Ktor call was wrong. Fixing those, plus more docs.










Only two errors remain — Kotlin smart-cast on cross-module properties. Fixing those.




Classic API-visibility problem: Room and Ktor types leak through public signatures. Fixing with `api` dependencies.




Build succeeded — the APK is generated. Now running the full unit-test suite.




Build and tests are green. Let me count the executed tests and make two small UI clean-ups.




58 tests, 0 failures; no device/emulator attached. Final verification of the build after the UI clean-ups.







## KONNEX — Android native foundation: delivered and verified

### 1. Files created

Nothing existed in the workspace except `.freebuff`; no user files were touched.

| Area | Files |
| --- | --- |
| Build system | `settings.gradle.kts`, `build.gradle.kts`, `gradle.properties`, `gradle/libs.versions.toml`, `local.properties`, `.gitignore`, wrapper (`gradle/wrapper/*`, `gradlew`, `gradlew.bat`), `build-logic/` (5 convention plugins + shared config) |
| 34 module build files | one `build.gradle.kts` + `AndroidManifest.xml` per module, `app/proguard-rules.pro` |
| Kotlin sources | **97 `.kt` files** (main + tests) |
| App resources | launcher adaptive icons (`mipmap-anydpi-v26`), original vector K mark, colours, window theme, strings |
| Documentation | **13 docs**: `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `STORAGE.md`, `BACKUP.md`, `SYNC_ENGINE.md`, `CALL_ENGINE.md`, `DATABASE_SCHEMA.md`, `API_SPEC.md`, `ANDROID_SPEC.md`, `OPENCODE_RULES.md` |
| Tooling | `scripts/build.sh`, `scripts/build.ps1` (pin JDK 17 + keep Gradle cache on D:) |
| Generated | `gradle/wrapper/gradle-wrapper.jar` (via Gradle 8.9 wrapper task), Room exported schema, `.logs/` build logs (git-ignored) |

### 2. Modules implemented (34, from `settings.gradle.kts`)

- **`:app`** — composition root (`AppContainer`), single NavHost, bottom bar, Application, manifest.
- **Core (7):** `core:common` (Outcome/typed errors, clock, ids, SHA-256, logger), `core:model`, `core:database` (Room, 9 tables, DAOs, transactions), `core:network` (Ktor client + API DTOs), `core:security` (Keystore AES-256-GCM, encrypted secret store, security report), `core:designsystem` (original palette/type/components/Canvas logo), `core:testing`.
- **Domain (7, pure JVM):** auth, messaging, contacts, storage, backup (archive format + manifest), sync (merge policy/planner), calling (reducer).
- **Data (5):** auth (DataStore prefs, profile/session, server gateway), messaging (Room repos, transactional outbox, demo seeder), contacts, media (checksum-verified attachments), storage (registry + persisted configuration).
- **Storage (4):** local (real, atomic + checksummed), google-drive (OAuth probe, transfers **not** implemented), nas (WebDAV client, unverified), remote-server (health + upload).
- **Infrastructure (3):** `:backup` (working engine + WorkManager), `:sync` (engine + cursor + worker), `:calling` (state holder + Android lifecycle contract).
- **Features (7):** onboarding (splash + 3-step), authentication (phone/OTP + local mode), chats, conversations, contacts, profile, settings (hub + **storage settings** + **backup settings**).
- **Deviations documented in `ARCHITECTURE.md`:** manual DI instead of Hilt (no annotation processing, one readable root), added `:data:storage`, JVM domain/core-network modules, settings sub-screens inside `:feature:settings`, `BackupScheduling` interface so features never touch WorkManager.

## KONNEX backend v0 — done, with the limits stated plainly

### What now exists

**Server (`server/`, standalone Gradle build, Kotlin 2.0.21 + Ktor 3.0.1 + SQLite)**
- `GET /v1/health`, OTP request/verify (documented dev provider returning `devCode`), session revoke
- `GET /v1/sync/pull`, `POST /v1/sync/push` — monotonic change stream, per-row revisions, idempotent push, tombstones, strictly increasing per-conversation `createdAt`
- `POST /v1/conversations/{id}/invite` (by KONNEX ID), `POST /v1/attachments`, `GET /v1/attachments/{id}`
- In-code idempotent migrations recorded in `schema_migrations`, membershi-scoped reads, hashed tokens/phone numbers, per-phone/per-address OTP limits, structured `{error:{code,message}}`, no request-body or token logging
- `Dockerfile` (non-root, healthcheck), `docker-compose.yml` (named volume), `server/README.md` with env-var table, Caddy/Nginx TLS proxy, hot/cold backup and restore

**Android**
- Settings → Server screen: URL validation (HTTPS except loopback/`10.0.2.2`), test connection, stored-token pill, sign-out; tokens in Keystore-encrypted storage, never logged
- Real sign-in against that server, dev code shown when the server offers one
- Outbox `QUEUED → SENT` only after the server accepts; `FAILED` after 8 failed attempts but the row stays queued; duplicate-safe retries
- New-conversation flow, invite dialog, long-press delete → server tombstone, SENT/QUEUED/FAILED message status
- Sync engine rebuilt on a `SyncStore` port (`RoomSyncStore` in production), push loop + paged pull, echo preservation, cursor persistence
- `network_security_config.xml` limits cleartext to dev hosts; version 0.2.0

## KONNEX — device validation, automatic sync, token refresh (committed)

### Phase 1 — Android device testing

**AVD configuration** (created because none existed; the SDK already had the image, so nothing was re-downloaded):

| Setting | Value |
|---|---|
| Name | `konnex_api35` (persists on disk) |
| System image | `system-images;android-35;google_apis;x86_64` (Android 15, API 35) |
| Device profile | `pixel_6`, 1080×2400 |
| Headless flags | `-no-window -no-audio -no-boot-anim -no-snapshot -gpu swiftshader_indirect` |

The emulator initially refused to boot (`x86_64 emulation currently requires hardware acceleration`). VT-x was enabled but no hypervisor existed, so I installed the Android Emulator Hypervisor Driver (`aehd` 2.2) from the SDK — this required your admin approval, which you granted. `emulator -accel-check` now reports *"AEHD (version 2.2) is installed and usable"*. **No physical phone was touched**; `ANDROID_SERIAL=emulator-5554` pinned the run to the emulator.

**Instrumentation results — 8/8 passed** (`:app:connectedDebugAndroidTest`, BUILD SUCCESSFUL):

```
AppStartupTest.applicationCreatesContainerAndMainActivityResumes
DatabasePersistenceTest.opensEveryTableAndPersistsAMessage
DatabasePersistenceTest.syncCursorSurvivesUpdatesAndNeverRegressesLocally
KeystoreStorageTest.cipherRoundTripUsesAKeystoreKey
KeystoreStorageTest.secretStoreEncryptsAtRestAndRemovesKeys
OutboxTransactionTest.creatingAConversationQueuesOneConversationChange
OutboxTransactionTest.sendingAMessageWritesMessagePreviewAndOutbox
OutboxTransactionTest.deletingAMessageWritesATombstoneWhenItWasSent
```

Two real failures were found and fixed, not worked around: a `ConversationEntity` constructor mismatch, and a genuine one — the Keystore test created a second `SecureSecretStore` over the same DataStore file, which fails by design ("multiple DataStores active for the same file"). The test now reuses the app's process-wide store, which is what production does; it also asserts the plaintext secret never appears in the on-disk preferences file.

### Phase 2 — Automatic sync

`SyncCoordinator` (`:sync`) decides *when*; `SyncEngine` still decides *what*.

- **After sign-in**: `AuthenticationRoute`'s continue callback triggers an immediate run and starts periodic work (only when a server session exists).
- **Foreground**: `ProcessLifecycleOwner.onStart` in `KonnexApplication`.
- **Connectivity recovery**: `registerDefaultNetworkCallback.onAvailable`.
- **Periodic**: WorkManager `konnex-sync`, 15-minute minimum, `CONNECTED`, exponential backoff from 30 s.
- **Battery discipline**: single-flight, one run per 20 s for automatic triggers (`SyncPolicy`, unit-tested), retries only for `Network`/`RateLimited` at 5 s → 15 s → 45 s then stop; no timers, no polling. A dead session (`Unauthorized`) is a terminal result, not a retry loop.
- Cursor persistence and duplicate-safe delivery unchanged and covered by existing tests; Settings still shows the live sync pill, last-sync time and queued count.

### Phase 3 — Token refresh

- `POST /v1/auth/refresh` with **atomic single-use rotation**: the presented refresh hash is part of the `UPDATE … WHERE`, so a replay or the loser of a concurrent pair gets 401; the old access token dies in the same statement. Access tokens now default to **60 minutes**; refresh lifetime 30 days, sliding.
- Client refreshes 2 minutes before expiry and once on any 401 (never in a loop), serialised by a mutex plus a generation check. A rejected refresh deletes the tokens and reports "Your session expired. Sign in again."
- Store: `sessionByRefreshHash` + `rotateSession`, migration **v2** (`idx_sessions_refresh`), per-IP refresh limiter.
- **Tests**: 6 server tests (`AuthRefreshTest`) and 4 client tests (refresh body/path, 401→refresh→retry with new token, rejected refresh not looped, no refresher = single attempt). Live check against a running server: 60.0-minute TTL, old access 401 after rotation, new access 200, replayed refresh 401, chained rotation 200, refresh after revoke 401.

### Phase 4 — Regression (all executed)

| Command | Result |
|---|---|
| `./gradlew :app:assembleDebug test` | **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** — 90 Android unit tests, 0 failures |
| `./gradlew -p server test` | **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** — 35 tests, 0 failures |
| `./gradlew :app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` | **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** |
| `ANDROID_SERIAL=emulator-5554 ./gradlew :app:connectedDebugAndroidTest` | **8/8 passed** |

**APK**: `app/build/outputs/apk/debug/app-debug.apk` — 20,075,490 bytes (19.1 MiB), installed on the emulator during the run.

### Phase 5 — Documentation & commit

Updated `README.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `SECURITY.md`, `SYNC_ENGINE.md`, `API_SPEC.md`, `server/README.md` (new endpoint, 60-minute TTL, scheduling rules, honest UI text that no longer claims rotation is missing). Documentation duties from `AGENTS.md` are covered for API, security and sync.

Committed as **`7f88a9d`** — *"Validate on an API 35 emulator and make sync automatic with token refresh"*; working tree is clean.

### Remaining limitations

1. **Two-device delivery is still unverified.** One emulator exists; I did not install on your two physical phones. Protocol-level two-client tests pass, plus the live HTTP run.
2. Sync is event-driven/periodic, not server-pushed (no FCM), so a backgrounded app may be up to 15 minutes behind.
3. No refresh-session management UI (you cannot list/revoke other devices), and a revoked refresh token means signing in again.
4. No E2EE and no database encryption at rest — unchanged, as instructed. No calling work started.
5. Emulator was shut down after the run; the AVD and AEHD driver remain installed for future runs.

## 2. Những gì đã chứng minh được

1. Nhắn tin hai chiều
   A gửi B, B trả lời A. Tin nhắn xuất hiện đúng một lần.
2. Offline rồi online
   Mất kết nối, tin nhắn vẫn ở QUEUED. Có mạng lại, tự gửi thành công.
3. Không gửi trùng
   Kiểm tra outbox, server change stream và cursor.
4. Gửi file
   TXT và PNG tải lên, tải xuống, checksum trùng khớp.
5. Đồng bộ nền
   B gửi tin khi A đang ở Home. A quay lại ứng dụng, tự nhận tin mà không cần bấm Sync.
6. Tương thích Android cũ và mới
   Đã kiểm tra Android 9 và Android 13.

Đặc biệt, việc sử dụng `adb reverse` giúp kiểm thử qua kết nối loopback mà không phải thay đổi cấu hình bảo mật hoặc mạng của điện thoại.

## 3. Có 3 việc cần lưu ý

- 9 test bị skip trong regression là các test harness cần tham số riêng, không phải 9 test thất bại.
- Chưa kiểm tra cuộc gọi đến khi khóa màn hình.
- Chưa có FCM nên chưa thể nhận thông báo tin nhắn tức thời khi app bị hệ điều hành giữ nền.

Ngoài ra, kiểm thử nhắn tin hiện tại chủ yếu được điều khiển bằng instrumentation, chưa phải kiểm thử thao tác UI thủ công.

## Part 2 — E2EE architecture and proposals (design only, nothing implemented)

Delivered (uncommitted, awaiting your approval):

| File | Contents |
|---|---|
| `E2EE_ARCHITECTURE.md` | Library evaluation, PQXDH + Double Ratchet, per-device sessions, envelope v2 wire format, multi-device fan-out, attachments, at-rest plan, compatibility, acceptance criteria |
| `KEY_MANAGEMENT.md` | Key inventory, generation/storage, rotation schedule, verification, backup options (none / passphrase archive / QR device linking), lost-device revocation, compromise recovery |
| `THREAT_MODEL.md` | Assets, 9 adversaries, what is/isn't protected, metadata leakage, residual risks, threat→test mapping |
| `E2EE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` | Server schema v2→v3 and Room v1→v2 migration DDL, Android module/integration proposal, 15-case two-phone test plan plus rollout/rollback |
| `SECURITY.md` (updated) | New "designed, not implemented" section; device-verification results; explicit `endToEndEncryptionImplemented = false` until criteria are met |
| `README.md` (updated) | Doc table, refreshed device rows, corrected stale "not run"/"no on-device proof" claims |

**Recommendation: libsignal (`org.signal:libsignal-android`)** — the only maintained Android-packaged implementation of PQXDH (Kyber1024 + X25519) and Double Ratchet, with per-device addressing and prekey/Kyber stores, 0.103.x–0.106.x released through late 2026, native libs for all four Android ABIs.

**Licence gate that needs your decision:** libsignal is **AGPL-3.0-only**, so a distributed KONNEX becomes an AGPL combined work. If KONNEX must stay proprietary, the fallback is **vodozemac (Apache-2.0)** — active, but no PQXDH and it needs our own Rust/UniFFI Android build. OpenMLS (MIT) is noted as the group-messaging path, not v1. Deprecated/legacy options (`libolm`, `libsignal-protocol-java`) are explicitly excluded. Your 2026 phone set matters here: Android 9 has no hardware Curve25519, which is why keys are wrapped with a Keystore AES key rather than expected from KeyStore.

Design guarantees written down: server stores only opaque envelopes (never bodies or attachment keys), per-file AES-256-GCM attachment keys travel inside the message, TOFU + safety numbers with a hard block on identity change, and honest limits (social-graph/timestamp metadata visible; a malicious server can withhold or replay; unlocked-device compromise is out of scope; SQLCipher is required before any at-rest claim).

No messaging code was changed, no dependencies added, and no E2EE claim is made anywhere. The proposal stops here for approval on: **licence choice**, SQLCipher scope, 1:1-only v1, and backup model. Say which way you want to go and I'll start with the flagged crypto module and the migration tests.

## 2. Mã hóa database – SQLCipher

Đề xuất chọn SQLCipher

Database hiện tại dùng Room + SQLite. Nếu chỉ mã hóa nội dung tin nhắn nhưng database vẫn để plaintext thì người lấy được file database vẫn có thể đọc nhiều thông tin riêng tư.

Mình muốn:

- Mã hóa toàn bộ SQLite database.
- Mã hóa khóa database bằng Android Keystore.
- Không lưu khóa dạng plaintext.
- Có migration và kiểm tra khả năng phục hồi.
- Không làm mất database hiện tại khi nâng cấp.

SQLCipher có hướng tích hợp với Room và hỗ trợ Android API 23 trở lên, phù hợp cả hai điện thoại Android 9 và Android 13 đã thử nghiệm.

## E2EE implementation readiness — evaluation and proposal complete

No code, dependency, schema change or protocol change was made. Everything below is documentation awaiting your approval.

### Deliverables (all in the repo, uncommitted)

| File | Size | Contents |
|---|---|---|
| `CRYPTO_LIBRARY_EVALUATION.md` | 11 KB | 12-criterion matrix, per-option findings, licence compliance report, verification gates |
| `DATABASE_ENCRYPTION.md` | 13.6 KB | SQLCipher vs alternatives, Keystore key hierarchy, migration algorithm, interrupt matrix, integrity checks, rollback |
| `ENCRYPTED_BACKUP.md` | 13.6 KB | Sealed format, key hierarchy, six destinations, passphrase + recovery key, restore/rotation, tests |
| `E2EE_ARCHITECTURE.md` | 22 KB | v1 scope, 4 diagrams, API changes, wire versioning, rollback matrix, acceptance criteria |
| `E2EE_IMPLEMENTATION_PLAN.md` | 17.8 KB | Server v2→v3 and Room v1→v2 migrations, Android module/integration plan, 15-case two-phone plan, phased rollout |
| `KEY_MANAGEMENT.md` | 10.8 KB | Key inventory incl. DB/backup keys, independence matrix, rotation, revocation, recovery |
| `THREAT_MODEL.md` | 10.4 KB | 10 adversaries, DB/backup-at-rest threats, residual metadata leakage, threat→test map |
| `SECURITY.md` | updated | "Designed, not implemented" sections, licence gate, device-verification results |

### 1. Library verdict

**libsignal** is the recommendation: it is the only maintained option with **PQXDH (Kyber1024 + X25519)** *and* **Double Ratchet**, official Android AAR (minSdk 23, four ABIs), and 0.103–0.106 releases through late 2026. Blocking conditions, stated plainly:

- **Licence:** declared AGPL-3.0-only in its POM. A distributed KONNEX becomes an AGPL combined work and must publish source. **The repo currently has no `LICENSE`/`NOTICE` file at all** — this must be resolved first. No commercial licence exists for libsignal.
- **Build risk:** the AAR is compiled to **Java 21** bytecode with desugaring; this build runs **JDK 17 / AGP 8.7.3**, so D8's acceptance of class-file v65 must be verified (V-1).
- **Fallback:** **vodozemac** (Apache-2.0) — real Double Ratchet, but **no PQXDH and no Kyber**: Olm's triple-DH handshake is explicitly *not* equivalent to Signal's PQXDH, and there is no official standalone Android artifact, so KONNEX would own a Rust/UniFFI build pipeline.
- **OpenMLS** (MIT) is documented as the future group path: MLS is epoch/tree-ratcheted, not Double Ratchet, and standard ciphersuites are not post-quantum.
- Rejected with reasons: libolm (deprecated 2024), libsignal-protocol-java (GPL, unmaintained), primitives-only libraries (would require designing a protocol ourselves), Proteus, OpenPGP.

Audit status is reported as found: protocol-level academic analyses exist for X3DH/Double Ratchet/PQXDH; **no complete public third-party audit of the libsignal implementation was found**; libolm had a 2016 NCC audit, vodozemac none that I could confirm.

### 2. Database encryption

SQLCipher Community (BSD-style) via Room `SupportOpenHelperFactory`; 32-byte random DB key in SQLCipher **raw-key** form, wrapped by a non-exportable Keystore AES-256-GCM key and stored outside the DB. Migration is export → `cipher_integrity_check` + `integrity_check` + row-count comparison → atomic swap with `.plain.bak` retained three verified opens → cleanup; a marker outside the DB makes every interruption point resumable or rollbackable. Includes WAL checkpointing to avoid plaintext residue, startup `cipher_version` assertion, and a rule that the plaintext DB is never deleted before a verified encrypted open.

**Decision needed:** current SQLCipher 4.20 pairs with `androidx.sqlite` 2.7/Room 3, while this project pins **Room 2.6.1**; either pin an older SQLCipher or schedule a Room upgrade milestone — `AGENTS.md` forbids a casual bump.

### 3. E2EE v1 scope

1:1 only, independent device identity, PQXDH session establishment, Double Ratchet (forward secrecy + post-compromise recovery), AEAD authentication, QR/safety-number verification, hard block on identity change, ciphertext-only server, per-file AES-GCM attachment keys. Multi-device is **prepared** (`deviceId` in schema, per-device envelopes, fan-out diagrams) but explicitly listed as deferred and not claimed. Wire format gains `protocolVersion` (1 = plaintext today, 2 = envelope), with capability advertisement and downgrade protection.

### 4. Backups

Streaming AES-256-GCM container: per-backup DEK wrapped by an Argon2id (64 MiB, t=3) passphrase KEK **and/or** a 160-bit recovery key via HKDF — so either unlocks, and changing a passphrase rewraps 48 bytes instead of re-encrypting the file. Backup keys are provably independent of identity keys, ratchet sessions and the SQLCipher key. Destinations: local SAF, Google Drive, NAS/SMB, WebDAV, SFTP, private KONNEX server — all sealed before upload; unconfigured destinations return `NotConfigured` instead of faking success. Restoring identity keys is off by default.

| On-device instrumentation | **not re-run this turn** — `connectedDebugAndroidTest` uninstalls the app afterwards and wiped the phones' data last turn; skipped to preserve data. The suite was green on both phones earlier this session (17/17 each). |
| Phones | `com.konnex.debug` still installed on both; no data touched this turn |

Secret scan of all eight docs: clean. Git history untouched (HEAD `0ba0c2c`); the docs are uncommitted — say the word and I'll commit them.

### Decisions required before any implementation

1. **Licence:** AGPL-3.0 for KONNEX (libsignal) or stay proprietary with vodozemac (no PQXDH)?
2. **SQLCipher vs Room 2.6.1:** pin older SQLCipher, or approve a Room upgrade milestone?
3. **JDK/toolchain:** approve verifying or upgrading for libsignal's Java 21 bytecode.
4. **Backup defaults:** attachments in/out, local vs server destination, identity-key restore allowed at all?
5. **Rollout order:** E2EE first, or database encryption first (it is protocol-independent and independently useful)?

I'm stopping here as instructed; no cryptography will be implemented until you approve.

## Database encryption readiness — audit complete, no code touched

New document: **`DATABASE_ENCRYPTION_IMPLEMENTATION_READINESS.md`** (503 lines) — findings, exact versions, per-file change list, state machine, failure matrix, security implications, 20 test cases, rollback, blockers, and a sign-off checklist.

### Answer to the central question

**Yes — SQLCipher works with Room 2.6.1 without upgrading Room**, by pinning:

```toml
net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android:4.13.0
```

Verified from POMs, not assumed: Room 2.6.1 requires `androidx.sqlite:sqlite:2.4.0`; SQLCipher 4.13.0 declares `2.2.0`, so Gradle resolves up to 2.4.0 and nothing moves. **4.14–4.17 force `sqlite` 2.6.2 and 4.18+ force 2.7.0 (Room 3)** — that is exactly why "SQLCipher 4.20 is compatible" is false for this project. Corroborated by Zetetic issue #81: Room 2.6.1 + SQLCipher built on sqlite 2.2 works; Room 2.8.4 crashes because SQLCipher lacks `beginTransactionReadOnly()`.

Also verified: 16 KB page support since 4.6.1 (Play requirement), ABIs `arm64-v8a/armeabi-v7a/x86/x86_64`, Community licence BSD-style, latest release 4.20.0 (2026-10-27) vs 4.13.0 (2026-01-20).

### Repository findings that change the plan

1. **The DB is a lazy process-wide singleton opened from a startup coroutine** (`AppContainer.database` → `KonnexApplication.onCreate`), and WorkManager **auto-initializes** (`androidx.startup.InitializationProvider` confirmed in the merged manifest) — so `SyncWorker`/`BackupWorker` can open the DB in a fresh process with no UI. Migration must therefore run **inside the first DB open**, behind a gate; a splash-screen migration alone is insufficient.
2. **No migrations exist yet** (Room v1, one exported schema, no destructive fallback) and there is **no raw SQLite/file access** anywhere — one builder call to change.
3. 9 entities / 9 DAOs, 15 DB consumers, 6 `withTransaction` call sites, all going through `RoomDatabase` → unaffected if the factory is wired once.
4. **`AndroidKeystoreCipher` only handles Strings** — it needs a byte-array wrap (or base64 reuse) plus an AAD-bound key for the DB key; today there is no rotation API.
5. **Backups are an unencrypted ZIP containing message bodies.** DB encryption would not protect them; that gap is now called out as a release condition, not a footnote.
6. Attachments live as plaintext files in `filesDir/attachments` — outside this milestone's coverage, also stated.

### Failure cases added beyond the earlier proposal

Keystore key **missing** vs **permanently invalidated** (separate paths), disk-full before rename, marker-lost-but-files-present (adopt state by content sniffing), `.bak` + encrypted DB both valid, double migration attempts, downgrade to a pre-SQLCipher APK (unreadable DB), worker-starts-before-migration, and the deliberate plaintext window — tightened from "keep `.plain.bak` for 3 opens" to **delete immediately after the first verified encrypted open**, since a lingering plaintext copy is worse than a rollback that user backup can cover.

### Verification run

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| `:app:assembleDebug test` | BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 90 unit tests, 0 failures |
| `-p server test` | BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 35 tests, 0 failures |
| `:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin` | BUILD SUCCESSFUL |
| Device tests | **Deliberately not run** — `connectedDebugAndroidTest` uninstalls the app and would erase phone data |
| Phones | `com.konnex.debug` still installed on both; no data, install or settings touched |
| Working tree | Only Markdown: the 7 earlier docs + this one (2 modified: `README.md`, `SECURITY.md`); **no build file, Kotlin file or schema changed**; nothing committed |

### Remaining blockers before coding

1. **SQLCipher 4.13.x support window** — it is ten months behind 4.20; either confirm Zetetic still ships fixes for it, or approve 4.14.1 + the `sqlite` 2.6.2 upgrade with full Room regression. This is the one item I could not settle from public sources.
2. Raw-key pass-through (`x'…'`) in `SupportOpenHelperFactory` 4.13.0 — must be proven by test D1, with passphrase-KDF fallback.
3. OpenSSL duplicate-library/APK-size check for the pre-LibTomCrypt build.
4. Approval of a blocking migration UX and the plaintext-window policy.
5. Acknowledgement that backups and attachments remain unprotected until their own milestones.

The approval checklist is §11 of the document — 15 items, ending with the explicit go-ahead to implement. I'm stopping here: no SQLCipher dependency, no code, no migration, no commit.

* done check/
I'll verify the version support policy, the actual key-handling source code, and the native payload of the 4.13.0 AAR before writing the gate.

A critical finding has emerged: SQLCipher 4.14.0 patched a **WAL-mode corruption bug** and strongly advised upgrades — which disqualifies 4.13.0 as a production pin. I'm verifying the replacement version and the raw-key path now.

## Verification gate: **NO-GO on 4.13.0** — conditional GO on 4.17.0 after a dependency spike

New report: **`SQLCIPHER_SECURITY_VERIFICATION.md`** (428 lines). No code, Gradle, schema, phone or commit changed. The previous readiness doc now carries a one-line "superseded on version choice" note; its audit and state machine remain current.

### The finding that changes the plan

Zetetic's 4.14.0 release notes state: SQLite is updated to 3.51.3, *"which fixes a critical WAL-reset database corruption bug. Applications running SQLCipher in WAL mode are strongly advised to upgrade."* **4.13.0 ships SQLite 3.51.2 — before that fix — and Room uses WAL by default.** Combined with Zetetic's support model (no Community LTS or backports; dedicated maintenance is a paid entitlement), the earlier 4.13.0 pin is not defensible.

### Version assessment

| | 4.13.0 | 4.14.1 | 4.17.0 | 4.20.0 |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SQLite | 3.51.2 | 3.51.3 | 3.53.3 | 3.54.1 |
| WAL corruption fix | **No** | Yes | Yes | Yes |
| `sqlcipher_export` sanitization | No | No | Yes | Yes |
| 4.19 advisory fixes | No | No | No | Yes |
| `androidx.sqlite` | 2.2.0 | 2.6.2 | 2.6.2 | 2.7.0 |
| Verdict | **rejected** | floor | **recommended, conditional** | after G0 |

**Recommended pin: `net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android:4.17.0`** — highest version on the `androidx.sqlite 2.6.2` line, and the fixes that matter for this codebase. Room stays 2.6.1, but `androidx.sqlite` must move 2.4.0 → 2.6.2, which pulls `sqlite-framework`, `sqlite-bundled`, `sqlite-ktx` and `kotlin-stdlib 2.1.20`.

### Raw-key verification

The Java layer passes the caller's `byte[]` **unchanged** to `nativeKey(connectionPtr, password)` — no encoding, quoting, hashing or KDF (`SupportOpenHelperFactory` and `SQLiteConnection` source read). Zetetic's API docs define `x'<64 hex>'` as a **raw 32-byte key with no key derivation**, and 96 hex as key + salt. So the intended design is supported. The JNI C shim itself was not read, so this is **partially verified**, with a non-destructive R1 test design (dedicated test DB name, header check, wrong-key check, raw-binary control) in §3.3.

### Native and packaging (measured, not assumed)

Downloaded and inspected the actual AARs:

- **One `.so` per ABI**, no `libcrypto`/`libssl` — LibTomCrypt is static, so no OpenSSL duplication.
- **16 KB pages verified from ELF headers**: every `PT_LOAD` has `p_align = 0x4000` on arm64-v8a, armeabi-v7a and x86_64.
- **Size**: AAR 4.01 MB; arm64 `.so` 2.10 MB (≈ +2.1 MB per install), universal ≈ +7.6 MB.
- **R8**: `proguard.txt` ships keep rules for `net.zetetic.database.**`, native methods and annotations.
- **Bytecode**: Java 17 (class major 61), matching the project toolchain.

### Migration safety verdict

Structure is sound; four hard changes are required:

1. **WAL is the dangerous part**: checkpoint → assert 0-byte WAL → delete `-wal`/`-shm` **before and after** the swap, or plaintext pages survive the migration.
2. **Deleting `.plain.bak` after the first encrypted open is insufficient.** Replace with a two-launch checkpoint: open + `cipher_integrity_check` + `quick_check` + row-count receipt → `CONFIRMED_PENDING_RESTART` → next cold start re-verifies → only then delete.
3. **Worker gating**: WorkManager auto-init is confirmed in the merged manifest, so `SyncWorker`/`BackupWorker` must return `Result.retry()` while migration is not `DONE`, in addition to the DB-open mutex.
4. **Startup ordering**: migration must precede the `KonnexApplication` coroutine that already touches the DB, with the UI shown before it blocks.

### Backup / attachment gates

Backups are currently a **plaintext ZIP with message bodies**, and attachments are **plaintext files** in `filesDir/attachments`. Six release gates in §6 prevent any "encrypted at rest" claim until those are covered or the claim is narrowed to "database only".

### Still unresolved (not dressed up as verified)

- Kotlin 2.0.21 reading Kotlin 2.1.20 metadata from `androidx.sqlite 2.6.2`.
- Room 2.6.1 runtime against `sqlite-framework 2.6.2`.
- Whether `sqlite-bundled` adds a second native SQLite to the APK.
- JNI C shim behaviour for `x'…'`.
- Real performance on the API-28 phone; OEM rename/fsync behaviour.
- Whether 4.20 + sqlite 2.7.0 is viable.

All six are itemised in §8 as U1–U10.

### Gate before implementation — G0 (not run)

An isolated probe module or throwaway branch: add 4.17.0 only there, run `:app:assembleDebug` on Kotlin 2.0.21 / AGP 8.7.3 / Room 2.6.1, record resolved versions, run R1 against a test-only DB, and inspect the APK for duplicate `.so` sets and size delta. **If G0 fails, the project must choose** between upgrading Kotlin/AGP/androidx as its own milestone, a different encryption approach, or accepting an unsupported library — none of which I recommend silently.

### Current state

- Working tree: documentation only (`README.md`, `SECURITY.md` modified earlier; 10 untracked docs including this one). No build file, Kotlin file or schema touched.
- Both phones still have `com.konnex.debug` installed; nothing installed, removed or reset.
- Nothing committed; HEAD still `0ba0c2c`.

The approval checklist is §9 of the report — blocking items are G0, accepting the `androidx.sqlite 2.6.2` move, the 4.17.0 pin (and its gap vs 4.19/4.20), the two-launch plaintext-deletion checkpoint, WAL handling, worker gating, the backup/attachment release gates, and the downgrade policy. Implementation starts only on your explicit go-ahead.

## G0 result: **GO for SQLCipher 4.17.0** — the two blockers are resolved by experiment

Report: **`SQLCIPHER_G0_COMPATIBILITY_REPORT.md`**. Production untouched: no source, Gradle, schema, dependency or phone change; nothing committed (HEAD `0ba0c2c`). The probe lived in `.logs/g0-probe/` (git-ignored) and ran on the API 35 emulator only; both phones still show `konnex.com`… sorry — still show exactly one `com.konnex.debug` package each and no probe package.

### The decisive measurements

**Dependency graph (no forcing):** Room stays 2.6.1; `androidx.sqlite` is pulled 2.4.0 → **2.6.2** by SQLCipher alone, and `kotlin-stdlib` 2.0.21 → **2.1.20**.

**Kotlin metadata (G0-B): PASS.** Kotlin 2.0.21 compiled a source file that deliberately touches `androidx.sqlite.SQLiteConnection`/`SQLiteStatement` (the Kotlin 2.1-built API). No metadata error, no skip flag. The one build failure was my own test using `use {}` on `RoomDatabase`, not a library issue.

**Room runtime (G0-C): PASS.** 3/3 instrumentation tests on `konnex_api35`, twice, including after a clean rebuild:

- encrypted create, insert, update, query, `withTransaction`
- `PRAGMA journal_mode` = **wal**
- close → reopen with same key → 3 rows intact
- `quick_check` = ok, `cipher_integrity_check` empty
- wrong key → `SQLiteNotADatabaseException` (code 26); original DB still opens
- random header `a5b3cda8…`, no plaintext in the file

**Packaging (G0-D):**

| | |
|---|---|
| SQLite engines in APK | **1** (`libsqlcipher.so` only; no `sqlite-bundled` native lib) |
| Native libs | 4 — one per ABI, no `libcrypto`/`libssl` |
| 16 KB alignment | **verified in the packaged APK**, `p_align = 0x4000` on all four ABIs |
| APK size | 1,498,855 B → **9,659,984 B**; delta **+8.16 MB universal**, **+2.10 MB per arm64 install** |
| R8 | `assembleRelease` with minify **and empty project rules**: success (AAR keeps its own rules) |
| Native loading | explicit `System.loadLibrary("sqlcipher")`, no `UnsatisfiedLinkError` |

**Raw key (G0-E): proven end-to-end.** 32-byte key passed as ASCII `x'…'` via `SupportOpenHelperFactory` opens a real Room DB with no KDF pause, rejects a different key, keeps the file encrypted, and reopens correctly.

### Why 4.20.0 is not the answer

`sqlcipher-android:4.20.0` failed AAR metadata:

> requires … compile against version **36** or later … maximum recommended compile SDK for AGP 8.7.3 is 35

The 4.18 release notes state compileSdk 37 for that line, so **4.18–4.20 are incompatible with the pinned toolchain**. 4.17.0 is the highest release that works with Room 2.6.1 / AGP 8.7.3 / Kotlin 2.0.21 — a ceiling, not a preference.

**Residual risk of 4.17.0:** it lacks the 4.19 advisory fixes (unquoted `sqlcipher_export` aliases, invalid `hexkey` URI) and 4.20's maintenance changes. The export advisory is not exploitable here **provided `sqlcipher_export` is only ever called with constant aliases** — that must be a code-review invariant. FTS5 CVEs are already fixed in 4.17 (SQLite 3.53.3); KONNEX uses no FTS.

### Still unresolved (honestly)

- **API 28 (LG-G710) behaviour** — not tested, because installing on the phones was prohibited.
- Real migration mechanics (export/swap/rename, Keystore wrap, process death, reboot, disk-full) — out of G0 scope.
- Minified **runtime** open (R8 build compiles; DB not opened in it).
- Performance on the API-28 phone.
- Plaintext backups and attachments remain the release-gate gaps from the previous phase.

### Approval checklist (implementation needs its own go-ahead)

- [ ] Accept 4.17.0 + `androidx.sqlite` 2.6.2 with Room 2.6.1 still pinned.
- [ ] Accept +8.16 MB universal / +2.10 MB per-device APK growth.
- [ ] Accept API 28 remains untested until phone installs are authorised.
- [ ] Accept the 4.19/4.20 gap (constant export aliases only), or schedule an AGP/compileSdk 36+ milestone for 4.20.
- [ ] Approve the two-launch migration checkpoint + WAL/SHM deletion.
- [ ] Approve backup/attachment release gates.
- [ ] Approve the non-destructive device-test procedure.
- [ ] **Explicit go-ahead to implement** — I have not started, and will not until you say so.

# Next Task: SQLCipher Migration Failure-Injection & Production Readiness

The SQLCipher G0 compatibility gate has passed.

Approved technical baseline for investigation:

- SQLCipher: `net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android:4.17.0`
- Room: `2.6.1`
- androidx.sqlite: `2.6.2`
- Kotlin: `2.0.21`
- AGP: `8.7.3`
- JDK: `17`

## Mission

Design and validate the complete plaintext SQLite → encrypted SQLCipher migration lifecycle before production implementation.

**This is an investigation and test-design task, NOT implementation.**

## Strict safety constraints

1. Do not modify production Kotlin/Java source.
2. Do not modify Gradle files, dependency versions, Room entities or schemas.
3. Do not change the database on either physical phone.
4. Do not install, uninstall, clear data or reset either phone.
5. Do not commit anything.
6. Use only disposable test projects and synthetic databases under `.logs/`.
7. Preserve all existing documentation and previous verification evidence.
8. Never use real user messages or production encryption keys in experiments.

## Phase 1 — Migration state machine

Specify a crash-safe state machine covering:

- Initial plaintext database.
- Migration requested.
- WAL checkpoint.
- Plaintext database export.
- Encrypted database creation.
- Integrity verification.
- Atomic replacement.
- First verified encrypted launch.
- Second cold-start verification.
- Plaintext backup deletion.
- Migration complete.

For every transition document:

- Preconditions.
- Files involved.
- Atomicity guarantees.
- Recovery action.
- User-visible state.
- Whether retry is safe.

## Phase 2 — Failure injection

Design and execute disposable tests for:

1. Process death during WAL checkpoint.
2. Process death during export.
3. Disk full during export.
4. Process death during encryption.
5. Corrupted encrypted temporary database.
6. Wrong SQLCipher key.
7. Missing Android Keystore key.
8. Permanently invalidated Keystore key.
9. Crash immediately before database rename.
10. Crash immediately after database rename.
11. Crash during marker update.
12. Missing or stale WAL/SHM files.
13. Concurrent worker attempting database access.
14. Two simultaneous migration attempts.
15. Device reboot during migration.
16. Recovery after interrupted migration.
17. Database downgrade attempt.
18. Both plaintext and encrypted copies appearing valid.

Use synthetic data and deterministic failure injection.

For each case record expected behavior and actual test results.

## Phase 3 — Android Keystore validation

Investigate:

- AES-256-GCM key generation.
- Non-exportability.
- AAD binding to application/database identity.
- Authentication and invalidation behavior.
- Backup/restore implications.
- Key loss recovery.
- API 28 compatibility.

Do not export or expose any real keys.

## Phase 4 — WorkManager and startup ordering

Verify the migration gate prevents:

- SyncWorker opening the database prematurely.
- BackupWorker opening the database prematurely.
- UI repositories accessing plaintext during migration.
- Multiple processes opening different database versions.

Provide a startup sequence diagram.

## Phase 5 — API 28 compatibility

Use an isolated emulator if available.

Validate:

- SQLCipher native library loading.
- Room database creation.
- WAL mode.
- Keystore AES-GCM operations.
- Encrypted reopen.
- Integrity checks.

Do not use either physical phone.

## Phase 6 — Performance and storage

Measure using synthetic databases:

- 10 MB.
- 50 MB.
- 100 MB.
- 250 MB.

Record:

- Migration duration.
- Peak temporary storage.
- Memory consumption.
- Encryption throughput.
- Startup blocking time.

Do not extrapolate measured values beyond the tested environment without clearly stating assumptions.

## Phase 7 — Backup and rollback interaction

Prove the migration cannot accidentally:

- Restore a plaintext database over an encrypted database.
- Delete the only recoverable copy.
- Leave plaintext WAL pages behind.
- Mistake a backup ZIP for an encrypted database.
- Claim full at-rest encryption while attachments and backups remain plaintext.

## Deliverable

Create:

`SQLCIPHER_MIGRATION_FAILURE_INJECTION_REPORT.md`

Include:

1. Executive verdict: GO / CONDITIONAL GO / NO-GO.
2. Architecture and state machine.
3. Sequence diagrams.
4. Failure-injection matrix.
5. Test evidence and reproducibility instructions.
6. API 28 compatibility results.
7. Performance measurements.
8. Unresolved risks.
9. Exact production file change list.
10. Rollback and recovery strategy.
11. Final approval checklist.

Update `SECURITY.md` only if necessary, without making claims that imply encryption has been implemented.

## Final rules

Clearly distinguish:

- Verified experimentally.
- Verified by source inspection.
- Reasoned but untested.
- Unknown.

Do not start implementation after completing this task.

Stop and report findings. Wait for explicit approval before modifying production code.

Vì sao nên làm bước này trước? G0 đã chứng minh SQLCipher hoạt động với toolchain hiện tại, nhưng chưa chứng minh migration an toàn khi app crash, mất điện, hết dung lượng hoặc Keystore mất key. Đây mới là rủi ro lớn nhất trước khi triển khai mã hóa database thật.

## Verdict: **CONDITIONAL GO** — report at `SQLCIPHER_MIGRATION_FAILURE_INJECTION_REPORT.md`

The migration design survived every failure I could actually inject. No tested failure lost a row, and none produced a silent plaintext fallback.

### What was actually executed (not just designed)

I built a disposable probe at `.logs/m1-probe/` and ran it against synthetic data only.

**18-test migration suite: 18/18 pass on API 35, and 18/18 again on API 28.** Covers happy path, wrong key, corrupt temp, stale WAL, missing Keystore key, downgrade, both-copies-valid, gate, two migrators, and 5 crash-point recoveries.

**Real process death, five times** — `Process.killProcess`/`halt`, then recovery in a fresh process:

| Killed at | Recovery |
|---|---|
| during checkpoint | DONE, 120 rows |
| mid-export (after first encrypted byte) | DONE, 300 rows |
| after encrypted rename | DONE, 140 rows, `.bak` deleted |
| during post-swap marker write | DONE, 110 rows |
| after marker SWAPPED | DONE, 130 rows |

**Real disk full:** 6 MB tmpfs inside the probe's own files dir, 3 MB database → `database or disk is full (code 1)`. Plaintext sha unchanged, 3 000 rows still readable, temp deleted.

**Device reboot mid-migration:** reboot 2 s into a 50 MB export (~6.4 s measured) → recovery DONE, 49 000 rows. Caveat: I could not capture the marker immediately before the reboot (adb shell wedged), so "mid-export" rests on timing, not a marker snapshot.

**Concurrency:** second migrator returns `ALREADY_RUNNING` both in-process and from a second `:mig` process; a worker blocks on the gate for 1 416 ms rather than opening anything.

**API 28 (isolated `konnex_api28` emulator, Android 9):** native load, `cipher_version=4.17.0`, Room create/insert/reopen (201 rows), WAL, both integrity checks, Keystore wrap/unwrap — 9/9 smoke+Keystore, plus the full migration suite 18/18. The physical LG-G710 was **not** touched.

**Keystore:** AES-256 non-exportable (`getEncoded() == null`), AAD mismatch and single-byte tamper both fail `AEADBadTagException`, key file holds only `keyId`+wrapped blob, missing/replaced alias → typed `MISSING_ALIAS`/`UNWRAP_FAILED`.

### Performance (x86_64 API 35 emulator, synthetic)

| Nominal | real bytes | migration | throughput | peak temp | mem | encrypted open |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 10 MB | 13.9 MB | 1 658 ms | 8.0 MB/s | 27.8 MB | 13.4 MB | 26 ms |
| 50 MB | 68.2 MB | 6 411 ms | 10.1 MB/s | 136 MB | 14.3 MB | 36 ms |
| 100 MB | 134.9 MB | 12 809 ms | 10.0 MB/s | 269 MB | 13.3 MB | 63 ms |
| 250 MB | 336.6 MB | 31 241 ms | 10.3 MB/s | 672 MB | 14.7 MB | 92 ms |

Peak temp ≈ 2× database size; memory stays flat. These are emulator numbers — the report says explicitly not to extrapolate to the LG-G710.

### Conditions before coding

1. Physical API 28 phone still untested (installs were prohibited); emulator passed.
2. `.plain.bak` must die in the same launch after the first verified encrypted open.
3. Keystore **invalidation is only mapped** — the real `KeyPermanentlyInvalidatedException` trigger needs biometric enrollment/lock change; auth-bound key creation is refused by the platform without one (`At least one fingerprint/biometric must be enrolled…`).
4. Attachments and backup payloads remain plaintext — proven on disk. The app must not claim full at-rest encryption until those ship.

Also unresolved: reboot during the two renames (killed at those points, not actually rebooted), R8 runtime, real Keystore on OEM hardware, 4.19 export-alias advisory.

### Safety

- `git status`: only Markdown (the new report plus the pre-existing uncommitted docs). No `.kt/.kts/.xml/.toml/.json` changed, nothing committed, HEAD still `0ba0c2c`.
- Both phones: still exactly one `com.konnex` package each, zero `m1probe`.
- `SECURITY.md` intentionally untouched — it already says the database is not encrypted and SQLCipher is planned; changing it now would imply progress that does not exist.
- Production change list is 19 file entries in §13, no entity/schema/version change.

Implementation has **not** started and will not until you approve.

# Next Task: SQLCipher Production Implementation — Phase 1

The following verification gates have passed:

- SQLCipher G0 compatibility: GO.
- Migration failure-injection: CONDITIONAL GO.
- API 28 emulator: 18/18 migration tests and 9/9 smoke + Keystore tests.
- Process-death recovery: all five tested scenarios passed.
- Performance: synthetic databases up to 250 MB tested.

Read these reports before starting:

- SQLCIPHER\_G0\_COMPATIBILITY\_REPORT.md
- SQLCIPHER\_MIGRATION\_FAILURE\_INJECTION\_REPORT.md

## Mission

Implement the foundational SQLCipher infrastructure in KONNEX.

This phase covers dependency integration, Keystore-backed database key management, and encrypted Room database creation.

Migration of existing production databases is explicitly OUT OF SCOPE.

## 1\. Strict boundaries

- Do not modify either physical phone.
- Do not uninstall, reinstall, clear app data or reset devices.
- Do not run connectedDebugAndroidTest against physical devices.
- Do not change existing production database files.
- Preserve all existing user data.
- Do not implement E2EE.
- Do not implement backup encryption or attachment encryption.
- Do not commit without approval.

Use the existing API 28 and API 35 emulators for verification.

## 2\. Dependency integration

Add the approved SQLCipher dependency:

`net.zetetic:sqlcipher-android:4.17.0`

Preserve:

- Room 2.6.1
- Kotlin 2.0.21
- AGP 8.7.3
- JDK 17

Verify the resolved dependency graph and prevent accidental upgrades.

Document the exact dependency changes.

## 3\. Database key management

Implement a dedicated production database key provider.

Requirements:

- Generate a cryptographically random 256-bit database key.
- Wrap it using Android Keystore AES-256-GCM.
- Keystore key must be non-exportable.
- Use authenticated additional data bound to the application and database identity.
- Never store the raw database key in preferences, logs, files or database records.
- Never derive the SQLCipher key from a password.
- Use the already verified SQLCipher raw-key format.
- Implement typed error handling for missing, invalidated and corrupted keys.
- Do not silently generate a replacement key when an existing encrypted database is present.

Keep database key management independent of E2EE identity and backup keys.

## 4\. Room integration

Integrate SQLCipher through `SupportOpenHelperFactory`.

Requirements:

- Preserve all existing DAO and repository APIs.
- Preserve existing transaction behavior.
- Preserve WAL mode.
- Ensure all database consumers use the same encrypted singleton.
- Assert the expected SQLCipher version at startup.
- Ensure database creation cannot silently fall back to plaintext SQLite.

Add a dedicated encrypted database test.

## 5\. Startup and worker safety

Review:

- KonnexApplication startup.
- AppContainer database initialization.
- WorkManager auto-initialization.
- SyncWorker.
- BackupWorker.

Ensure workers cannot access a partially initialized or incorrectly keyed database.

Do not introduce a migration gate that pretends existing plaintext databases are encrypted.

Until Phase 2 migration exists, handle the existing plaintext production database explicitly and safely.

## 6\. Security tests

Add tests covering:

1. New database creation.
2. Random key generation.
3. Keystore wrapping and unwrapping.
4. Encrypted database header.
5. Correct-key reopen.
6. Wrong-key rejection.
7. Missing Keystore alias.
8. Corrupted wrapped key.
9. AAD mismatch.
10. WAL operation.
11. Room transactions.
12. No plaintext fallback.
13. Key independence from backup and identity keys.

Use disposable database names and synthetic records.

## 7\. Build and verification

Run:

- `:app:assembleDebug`
- `:app:testDebugUnitTest`
- `:app:compileDebugAndroidTestKotlin`
- `:app:assembleRelease`
- SQLCipher instrumentation tests on API 28 emulator.
- SQLCipher instrumentation tests on API 35 emulator.

Verify:

- R8/minification.
- Native library loading.
- 16 KB alignment.
- APK size change.
- No duplicate SQLite native engines.
- No secrets in logs or generated artifacts.

## 8\. Critical migration boundary

The existing production database is plaintext.

Do not point the new encrypted database configuration at the existing production database.

Do not delete, rename, overwrite or migrate it in this phase.

The application must fail safely rather than opening the existing database with an unrelated new key.

If safe coexistence is impossible without implementing migration, stop and report the blocker instead of bypassing it.

## 9\. Documentation

Create:

`SQLCIPHER_PHASE1_IMPLEMENTATION_REPORT.md`

Include:

- Exact source files changed.

Update SECURITY.md only with claims that are experimentally verified.

## 10\. Completion criteria

Phase 1 is complete only when:

- New test databases are encrypted.
- Existing production data remains untouched.
- No plaintext fallback is possible.
- Keystore key handling passes all security tests.
- API 28 and API 35 tests pass.
- Release build succeeds.

Stop after Phase 1.

Do not begin plaintext-to-encrypted production migration.

Do not implement backup or attachment encryption.

Do not implement E2EE.

Report the results and wait for explicit approval before Phase 2.

**What now works**

Audit complete: `SQLCIPHER_PHASE1_5_SECURITY_AUDIT.md` (379 lines). No production code, schema or cryptographic logic was changed, nothing was committed, and no physical phone was touched.

**Verdict: crypto core passes; release is blocked by two High findings.**

**High**
1. **Database-unavailable crashes the app.** With the key file deleted (or its wrapped value corrupted), startup logs `KEY_MISSING`/`UNAVAILABLE`, then composition accesses `profileRepository` → `AppContainer.database` and throws on the main thread: `FATAL EXCEPTION: main … DatabaseSecurityException`. No recovery screen, no typed UI state, no export/reset path. Restoring the key file brought the data back intact.
2. **Downgrade split-brain.** Pre-Phase-1 APK → launch created a plaintext `konnex.db` while leaving `konnex-encrypted.db` untouched. Reinstalling Phase 1 then preferred the legacy file, silently running plaintext and hiding the encrypted data. Deleting the synthetic legacy DB restored the encrypted data. There is no dual-file guard; this also re-enables plaintext storage.

**Medium**
- No recoverable backup/export: `allowBackup=false` is correct, but the Keystore alias is install-bound, so restore always ends in `MISSING_ALIAS`.
- Raw key is copied into an immutable hex `String` and never zeroized.
- `SecurityInspector` swallows open failures; Settings/Profile then display **"Planned — not implemented"/"Planned"** on a broken encrypted install.

**Low:** key-creation race (not reachable with today's single container), imprecise error typing (malformed Base64 → `UNAVAILABLE`; replaced DB → `VERIFICATION_FAILED`; Keystore unavailable → `MISSING_ALIAS`), failed fresh creation leaves `-wal`/`-shm`, rename fallback can delete a valid key, unused key-file version/no rotation, global `DatabaseAccess` state, leftover wrapped test key files.

**Verified good:** 32-byte random key, non-exportable AES-256-GCM wrapping with identity AAD, distinct alias from the secret store, no raw key storage, typed refusal on missing/wrong/corrupt key, plaintext-at-encrypted-path refusal, encrypted WAL/SHM with no plaintext body, `allowBackup=false`, no key material in logcat, v2→v3 update preserving key and data, truncated APK update rejected with the app intact.

**Compatibility matrix:** Phase1→legacy unsafe (creates plaintext shadow); legacy→Phase1 unsafe (split-brain); Phase1→newer Phase1 safe; interrupted update safe; missing key fail-closed but crashes; replaced encrypted DB fail-closed but mis-typed. Recommendation: do not ship Phase 1; minimum safe release is the build containing the dual-file guard and availability gate, ideally with server-side minimum-client enforcement.

**Regression (all green):** 116 unit tests / 22 suites, 35 server tests / 7 suites, 18/18 instrumentation on both API 35 and API 28, debug + release + R8 release builds. `DeviceSyncTest`'s 9 scripted steps were not run (require a live server/args); full unfiltered `connectedDebugAndroidTest` was not run. No flakiness observed, but each suite ran once per device.

The report includes the full database-access inventory, key-lifecycle analysis, UI failure design, severity table, exact proposed per-file changes, and a Phase 2 entry checklist. Fixes were deliberately not applied — tell me whether to implement F-01/F-02 (and which lower items) before re-audit, or proceed differently.
