## 2. Bốn vấn đề mình đặc biệt quan tâm

### 1. License của libsignal – U-01

Đây là vấn đề cần giải quyết đầu tiên.

Nếu KONNEX dự kiến phát hành thương mại hoặc mã nguồn đóng, AGPL-3.0 có thể tạo ra nghĩa vụ pháp lý không phù hợp.

Không nên tự ý sử dụng libsignal trước khi:

- Xác định license của toàn bộ sản phẩm.
- Đánh giá nghĩa vụ AGPL.
- Xác định khả năng xin license thương mại hoặc lựa chọn implementation khác.

### 2. OpenMLS – U-02

OpenMLS là lựa chọn đáng xem xét cho group messaging.

Nhưng cần xác minh:

- Android FFI thực tế.
- UniFFI integration.
- Rust toolchain.
- Khả năng build reproducible.
- Dependency pinning.
- Memory safety tại ranh giới Kotlin/Rust.

Không được chỉ dựa vào việc thư viện hỗ trợ Rust để kết luận Android đã sẵn sàng.

### 3. Device revocation – U-14b

HIGH PRIORITYĐây là vấn đề kiến trúc rất quan trọng.

Nếu tài khoản bị thu hồi một thiết bị nhưng bearer token của thiết bị đó vẫn hợp lệ, kẻ đang kiểm soát thiết bị có thể tiếp tục truy cập server.

Cần xử lý device-bound authorization trước khi phát hành E2EE.

### 4. Backup archive schema – U-11

Phase 2.6 hiện từ chối các entry prefix không nhận diện.

Nếu thêm E2EE metadata vào backup mà không xử lý compatibility, có thể phá vỡ khả năng khôi phục backup cũ.

Phải giải quyết theo hướng tương thích ngược, không được làm hỏng KONNXBK2 hiện tại.

## 3. Thứ tự triển khai mình đề xuất

Không nên chuyển thẳng sang Phase 3.1. Nên có một phase chuẩn bị kỹ thuật:

1. Phase 3.0 – Architecture Design
   Hoàn thành9 tài liệu thiết kế E2EE.
2. Phase 3.0.1 – Protocol & Dependency Qualification
   Tiếp theoĐóng U-01, U-02 và đánh giá các vấn đề kiến trúc quan trọng.
3. Phase 3.1 – Device Identity & Key Management
   Tạo danh tính thiết bị, đăng ký khóa, xác minh và thu hồi thiết bị.
4. Phase 3.2 – One-to-One E2EE
   PQXDH, Double Ratchet, encrypted envelopes.
5. Phase 3.3 – Multi-Device
   Đồng bộ và quản lý session giữa các thiết bị.
6. Phase 3.4 – Group E2EE
   MLS, group membership và epoch management.
7. Phase 3.5 – E2EE Attachments & Recovery
   Mã hóa file đầu cuối và tích hợp backup.
8. Phase 3.6 – E2EE Security Verification
   Kiểm thử, interoperability và security review.

## 4\. Khuyến nghị quan trọng

Mình muốn giữ ba nguyên tắc cho KONNEX:

- Không tự phát minh cryptographic protocol.
- Không để server có quyền khôi phục hoặc thay thế private key của người dùng.
- Không hy sinh khả năng tương thích đa nền tảng để triển khai nhanh Android trước mắt.

Đặc biệt, cần phân biệt rõ hai việc: backup lịch sử tin nhắn và backup khóa E2EE. Hai thứ này không nên tự động gộp làm một.

## 5\. Bước tiếp theo

Mình đề xuất viết ngay prompt:

Phase 3.0.1 – E2EE Protocol Qualification & Dependency Security Review

Phase này sẽ yêu cầu OpenCode:

1. Đánh giá phương án xử lý AGPL của libsignal.
2. Đánh giá OpenMLS và UniFFI trên Android.
3. Giải quyết U-11 và U-14b ở mức thiết kế/implementation tùy phạm vi được duyệt.
4. Kiểm tra 7 câu hỏi bảo mật còn bỏ ngỏ.
5. Xác định bộ thư viện và phiên bản chính xác trước khi viết E2EE.
6. Chỉ bắt đầu Phase 3.1 khi các blocker bắt buộc đã được đóng.

Mình không khuyên bỏ qua hai blocker U-01 và U-02. Đây là những quyết định có thể ảnh hưởng đến toàn bộ kiến trúc và khả năng thương mại hóa KONNEX sau này.



Tiếp theo là Phase 3.0.1 – E2EE Protocol Qualification & Dependency Security Review.

Đây là phase trung gian để giải quyết các blocker của Phase 3.0 trước khi xây dựng Device Identity và Key Management.

Mình thiết kế prompt theo hướng: OpenCode phải nghiên cứu thư viện thực tế, kiểm tra license, khả năng tích hợp Android/Rust, đánh giá các quyết định mật mã và đưa ra lựa chọn có bằng chứng. Không được tự chọn thư viện chỉ vì nó dễ tích hợp.

Nguyên tắc: Không triển khai E2EE production trong Phase này. Riêng U-14b có thể được sửa nếu audit xác nhận lỗi và việc sửa không gây breaking change.

## Prompt Phase 3.0.1

Writing

# Phase 3.0.1 — E2EE Protocol Qualification & Dependency Security Review

Perform a rigorous technical, legal, cryptographic, and platform-compatibility qualification of the E2EE architecture designed in Phase 3.0.

This phase must resolve implementation blockers before Phase 3.1.

**Phase 3.0.1 is primarily an audit and qualification phase. Do not implement the production E2EE protocol.**

The goal is to select exact, maintainable, legally compatible cryptographic implementations and establish an evidence-based implementation baseline.

Phase 2.7 remains skipped.

---

# 1\. Initial Repository Audit

Inspect the actual current repository and working tree.

Read:

- All nine Phase 3.0 design documents.
- `E2EE_PHASE3_0_REPORT.md`.
- `E2EE_PROTOCOL_COMPARISON_PHASE3_0.md`.
- `E2EE_PROTOCOL_SPEC_PHASE3_0.md`.
- `E2EE_KEY_MANAGEMENT_PHASE3_0.md`.
- `E2EE_MULTI_DEVICE_PHASE3_0.md`.
- `E2EE_GROUP_MESSAGING_PHASE3_0.md`.
- `E2EE_BACKUP_RECOVERY_INTEGRATION_PHASE3_0.md`.
- `E2EE_THREAT_MODEL_PHASE3_0.md`.
- Existing Gradle and version catalog configuration.
- Existing server implementation.
- Authentication and account session management.
- Existing database entities and migrations.
- Backup and restore implementation.
- Android Keystore and Tink integration.

Verify every unresolved item U-01 through U-19 against the actual source.

Do not assume previous reports accurately describe the current working tree.

Create a traceability matrix connecting:

- Original finding.
- Current implementation.
- Required evidence.
- Qualification decision.
- Remaining blocker.

# 2\. U-01 — libsignal License Qualification

Investigate the actual licensing implications of integrating libsignal.

Inspect:

- Official repository license.
- License files.
- Vendored dependencies.
- Build artifacts.
- Android native integration.
- Distribution requirements.
- Commercial use implications.
- Source disclosure obligations.
- Application licensing constraints.

KONNEX has not yet declared a definitive product license.

Do not assume that merely using a library as a dependency automatically resolves all licensing obligations.

Evaluate three options:

A. Adopt AGPL-compatible product licensing.

B. Obtain appropriate commercial licensing or written legal authorization.

C. Select an alternative established implementation with compatible licensing.

Provide a decision matrix covering:

- Security maturity.
- Protocol completeness.
- Maintenance.
- Android integration.
- Multi-platform support.
- Licensing.
- Commercial distribution.
- Long-term risk.

Do not remove or modify licensing notices.

Do not claim legal advice or legal approval.

If the license cannot be resolved, mark U-01 BLOCKED.

**Acceptance:** No libsignal production integration until the licensing decision is explicitly approved.

# 3\. U-02 — OpenMLS Android Qualification

Investigate OpenMLS using its actual source repository and official documentation.

Verify:

- Exact stable release.
- License.
- Rust toolchain requirements.
- Android NDK compatibility.
- `aarch64-linux-android`.
- `armv7-linux-androideabi`, if required.
- `x86_64-linux-android` for emulators.
- JNI/UniFFI compatibility.
- Memory ownership.
- Error propagation.
- Thread safety.
- Storage provider requirements.
- Serialization stability.
- Group-state persistence.
- Key storage.
- R8 compatibility.
- APK/AAB packaging.

Do not assume an Android target compiling in CI means the complete Android integration works.

Investigate whether the previously proposed minimum version of 0.9.2 is still appropriate.

Verify actual releases and security fixes rather than blindly accepting an old version requirement.

Evaluate at least:

- Direct UniFFI integration.
- A narrow custom JNI boundary.
- A maintained alternative MLS implementation.

Do not introduce a new binding framework without justification.

Produce a platform compatibility matrix.

If OpenMLS cannot be qualified, provide an alternative architecture.

# 4\. U-03 Through U-19 — Full Unresolved Decision Review

Review every unresolved item individually.

For each U-ID provide:

- Original question.
- Actual current evidence.
- Security implications.
- Available alternatives.
- Recommended decision.
- Required implementation phase.
- Acceptance criteria.
- Whether the decision blocks Phase 3.1.

Pay special attention to:

- U-11: Backup archive schema compatibility.
- U-14b: Device-bound authorization and revocation.
- Header hash binding.
- MLS epoch retention.
- Transparency architecture.
- Device signing key reuse.
- Streaming attachment framing.
- Historical message sharing.
- Session rollback.
- Device trust.
- Recovery behavior.

Do not silently close an unresolved item without evidence.

# 5\. U-11 — Backup Archive Compatibility

Audit the Phase 2.6 archive reader.

Confirm the actual behavior when encountering unknown entry prefixes.

Design a backward-compatible extension mechanism.

Requirements:

- Existing KONNXBK2 backups remain readable.
- Unknown optional metadata can be handled safely.
- Unknown mandatory security-critical entries must fail closed.
- No unrecognized entry can bypass archive validation.
- No private E2EE keys are added to existing backups during this phase.
- Existing restore semantics remain intact.

If an implementation fix is necessary, first establish whether it is safe and whether it changes existing archive behavior.

Any modification must have dedicated regression tests for legacy backups.

Do not modify the backup container format.

# 6\. U-14b — Device-Bound Server Authorization

This is a high-priority security issue.

Audit the actual server authentication flow.

Determine whether:

- Account sessions are bound to a device UID.
- Revoked devices can continue using bearer tokens.
- Refresh tokens remain valid after device revocation.
- Existing sockets remain authenticated after revocation.
- Message delivery checks current device trust.
- Device removal invalidates all related credentials.

If the finding is confirmed, implement a narrowly scoped remediation.

Required properties:

1. Every authenticated device session has a server-verifiable device identity.
2. Revocation prevents further authenticated operations.
3. Refresh tokens cannot silently recreate revoked sessions.
4. Existing WebSocket or equivalent connections are invalidated or rechecked.
5. Device revocation is auditable.
6. Normal sessions continue working.
7. Existing authentication compatibility is preserved where safe.

Add tests for:

- Two devices on one account.
- Revoke one device.
- Continue using the other device.
- Revoked bearer token.
- Revoked refresh token.
- Existing socket.
- Concurrent revocation and request.
- Re-registration of a revoked device.
- Replay of old device credentials.

Do not redesign the complete authentication system.

Do not weaken authentication to simplify migration.

If a safe fix requires a breaking change, document it and leave the implementation blocked pending an explicit decision.

# 7\. Cryptographic Implementation Qualification

Evaluate the proposed cryptographic stack.

Direct messaging:

- PQXDH.
- Double Ratchet.
- Sesame or an equivalent multi-device architecture.

Group messaging:

- MLS RFC 9420.
- OpenMLS or a qualified alternative.

Evaluate:

- Cryptographic security.
- Forward secrecy.
- Post-compromise security.
- Post-quantum security.
- Device authentication.
- Cross-platform interoperability.
- Session persistence.
- State rollback.
- Library maintenance.
- Independent security audits.

Do not confuse post-quantum key establishment with post-quantum authentication.

Document exactly which guarantees are provided by each selected protocol.

Do not invent hybrid cryptographic compositions.

# 8\. Seven Architectural Questions

Resolve the seven questions identified in Phase 3.0:

1. Header hash binding versus outer AEAD.
2. MLS epoch retention.
3. Chain-only transparency versus a global transparency log.
4. Reuse of DIK\_sig as an MLS leaf signing key.
5. STREAM framing versus Tink keyset on the wire.
6. Whether HISTORY\_SHARE belongs in version 1.
7. Whether U-14b must be closed before encrypted messaging ships.

For each question provide:

- Security analysis.
- Recommended approach.
- Alternatives.
- Compatibility implications.
- Decision record.

Do not leave important cryptographic choices as implicit assumptions.

# 9\. Cross-Platform Qualification

Evaluate the eventual shared protocol layer for:

- Android.
- iOS.
- Windows.
- Linux.

Identify:

- Required native libraries.
- Required toolchains.
- Binary distribution requirements.
- Canonical serialization.
- Byte ordering.
- Integer encoding.
- Version negotiation.
- Cryptographic test vectors.
- Interoperability testing.

Do not introduce platform-specific cryptographic message formats.

Android-specific Keystore functionality must remain outside the shared wire protocol.

# 10\. Dependency and Supply-Chain Security

Evaluate the exact dependencies proposed for E2EE.

Include:

- License compatibility.
- Known vulnerabilities.
- Maintenance activity.
- Release cadence.
- Transitive dependencies.
- Native build reproducibility.
- Dependency pinning.
- Integrity verification.
- SBOM requirements.

Do not silently upgrade existing dependencies.

Do not add dependencies during this phase unless required for a narrowly scoped U-14b fix.

Create a recommended dependency bill of materials with exact versions and evidence.

# 11\. Controlled Implementation Scope

This phase is primarily design and qualification.

Allowed:

- Documentation changes.
- Architecture decision records.
- Dependency qualification.
- Tests for confirmed authentication defects.
- A narrowly scoped U-14b server authorization fix.
- A narrowly scoped U-11 compatibility fix, only if necessary and proven safe.

Forbidden:

- E2EE production messaging.
- Device cryptographic identity implementation.
- PQXDH implementation.
- Double Ratchet implementation.
- MLS implementation.
- Group encryption.
- E2EE attachment implementation.
- E2EE key backup.
- Changes to KONNXBK2 format.

Do not modify unrelated modules.

# 12\. Required Deliverables

Create:

1. `E2EE_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_0_1_AUDIT.md`
2. `E2EE_LIBRARY_LICENSE_REVIEW_PHASE3_0_1.md`
3. `E2EE_OPENMLS_ANDROID_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_0_1.md`
4. `E2EE_UNRESOLVED_DECISIONS_PHASE3_0_1.md`
5. `E2EE_DEPENDENCY_BOM_PHASE3_0_1.md`
6. `E2EE_CROSS_PLATFORM_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_0_1.md`
7. `E2EE_PHASE3_0_1_REPORT.md`

Update existing Phase 3.0 documents only when necessary.

Preserve historical decisions and explain superseded decisions instead of silently rewriting history.

# 13\. Testing Requirements

If U-14b or U-11 implementation changes are made, run relevant tests.

Run:

- Full JVM tests.
- Full server tests.
- Android unit tests.
- API 28 instrumentation.
- API 35 instrumentation.
- Existing backup compatibility tests.
- Authentication regression tests.
- Debug build.
- Release build.
- R8 release build.

Do not claim tests passed unless actual results are available.

Do not claim an Android native library is compatible based only on theoretical analysis.

# 14\. Git and Safety Constraints

- Do not commit.
- Do not push.
- Do not change branches.
- Do not reset.
- Do not clean the working tree.
- Preserve all pre-existing uncommitted changes.
- Do not modify existing backups.
- Use disposable emulators.
- Do not use physical phones.
- Do not change dependency versions without approval.
- Do not implement production E2EE.

Report exact files changed.

# 15\. Final Qualification Gate

Produce an explicit status for every blocker:

- RESOLVED
- APPROVED
- BLOCKED
- DEFERRED
- NOT VERIFIED

Phase 3.0.1 may be marked complete as a qualification phase even if some decisions remain blocked, but it must not declare Phase 3.1 ready unless all mandatory blockers are resolved.

Mandatory gates:

1. License strategy for direct messaging is established.
2. MLS implementation is technically qualified.
3. U-11 compatibility strategy is established.
4. U-14b is fixed or formally proven not applicable.
5. Cryptographic composition is coherent.
6. Cross-platform architecture is viable.
7. No unresolved decision silently undermines the security model.

If licensing or implementation feasibility remains unresolved, provide a revised protocol recommendation rather than forcing the original Phase 3.0 selection.

End with one of:

- APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.1
- APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS
- NOT APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.1

**Begin with repository audit. Establish evidence before making changes. Do not implement E2EE production functionality.**

- [OpenMLS – README](<https://github.com/openmls/openmls/blob/main/README.md?utm_source=chatgpt.com>) — hỗ trợ Rust và các target Android.
- [OpenMLS releases](<https://github.com/openmls/openmls/releases?utm_source=chatgpt.com>) — kiểm tra phiên bản thực tế.
- [PQXDH specification](<https://signal.org/docs/specifications/pqxdh/?utm_source=chatgpt.com>) — giao thức thiết lập khóa.
- [Double Ratchet specification](<https://signal.org/docs/specifications/doubleratchet/?utm_source=chatgpt.com>) — forward secrecy và post-compromise security.

Lưu ý: Các thông tin trên là điểm xuất phát cho việc thẩm định; OpenCode vẫn phải kiểm tra trạng thái repository, release và license tại thời điểm thực hiện.

## Phase 3.0.1 — complete

**Verdict: NOT APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.1.** Qualification itself passed; exactly one mandatory gate is unresolved and it is not an engineering decision:

- **U-01 — BLOCKED.** libsignal is AGPL-3.0-only, the current Rust repo carries no §7 App-Store permission, and there is no commercial programme. Option A (accept AGPL) still does not clear the iOS App Store; Option C (permissive 1:1 ratchet such as vodozemac) clears licensing only by withdrawing every post-quantum claim. Answer §14 item 1 and this flips to *APPROVED* (A/B) or *APPROVED (Option C)*.

## Test evidence (all actually run)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Server suite | BUILD SUCCESSFUL — 46 tests, 0 failures |
| Root JVM suite | BUILD SUCCESSFUL — 406 tests, 0 failures (57 classes) |
| Debug / Release / R8 | all BUILD SUCCESSFUL; `minifyReleaseWithR8`, `mapping.txt` produced |
| API 35 (emulator-5554) | **OK (105 tests)**, 423.8 s; kill run → `Process crashed.` (by design); recovery → **OK (1 test)** |
| API 28 (emulator-5556) | **OK (105 tests)**, 287.7 s; kill run → `Process crashed.` (by design); recovery → **OK (1 test)** |
| Backup compatibility | green on both APIs (`EncryptedBackupTest` 13, `FreshInstallRecoveryTest` 19, `StreamingBackupInstrumentedTest` 3, `DatabaseMigrationTest` 20) |

107 tests across 13 classes per API. The 9 non-passes per run are `DeviceSyncTest` assumption-skips that need `-e phone` — reported as skipped, not passed. `ProcessDeathRestoreTest` is necessarily run as separate invocations because it SIGKILLs its own process.

## What changed in the tree

- **Seven new documents**: audit, licence review, OpenMLS/Android qualification, unresolved decisions (U-01…U-19 + QDR-01…07 + U-11 rules), dependency BOM, cross-platform qualification, and the report.
- **One code fix — U-14b only**: server schema v3 with a device registry, device-bound sessions, per-device revocation and legacy backfill (`KonnexStore.kt`, `Routes.kt`, `Validation.kt`, `Dtos.kt`, `ServerModule.kt`), the client device header and device endpoints (`KonnexApiClient.kt`, `ServerConnectionManager.kt`, `AppContainer.kt`), and a new 11-test `DeviceAuthorizationTest.kt`.
- **Five docs updated**, with superseded decisions explained rather than rewritten: `API_SPEC.md`, `SECURITY.md`, and targeted notes in `E2EE_ARCHITECTURE_PHASE3_0.md`, `E2EE_MULTI_DEVICE_PHASE3_0.md`, `E2EE_GROUP_MESSAGING_PHASE3_0.md`.

No E2EE messaging, identity, PQXDH, ratchet, MLS, group, attachment or key-backup code was written; no `KONNXBK2` byte changed; no dependency version moved; no commit, push, branch change, reset or clean (HEAD still `1d1b98d`, 96 uncommitted entries preserved). Only disposable emulators were used — the two attached physical phones were never touched.

**Not verified, and said so in the report:** U-02 Android packaging (no Rust/NDK artifact exists, so no native-compatibility claim is made), U-15 API 26 Ed25519, cross-version archive compatibility (schema-3 `entryClass` mechanism and B3-1…B3-10 belong to Phase 3.4), dependency integrity/SBOM, and all non-Android platforms.

Vậy tôi đã hiểu rõ định hướng KONNEX. Tôi đề xuất chốt mô hình phát triển như sau:

# KONNEX

100% Free

Open Source · Community-driven · Privacy-first

# Miễn phí hoàn toàn

- Không thu phí tải ứng dụng.
- Không thu phí sử dụng tính năng.
- Không quảng cáo ở giai đoạn đầu.
- Công khai mã nguồn.
- Cho phép cộng đồng đóng góp.
- Hỗ trợ tự triển khai server.
- Ưu tiên quyền riêng tư và E2EE.
  Quảng cáo có thể được xem xét trong tương lai, không phải yêu cầu của phiên bản hiện tại.

## 1. Quyết định giấy phép

Với định hướng này, tôi đề xuất:

- AGPL-3.0: ứng viên cho giấy phép toàn bộ KONNEX nếu bạn muốn cả client và server đều phải công khai mã nguồn khi phân phối hoặc cung cấp dịch vụ mạng thuộc phạm vi giấy phép.
- GPL-3.0: phù hợp nếu muốn nghĩa vụ copyleft chủ yếu gắn với việc phân phối phần mềm.
- Không cần mua commercial license chỉ vì ứng dụng miễn phí hoặc public source.

Lưu ý: AGPL không cấm quảng cáo hay kiếm tiền sau này. Tuy nhiên, cần kiểm tra riêng tương thích giấy phép giữa các thư viện và điều khoản phân phối ứng dụng.

Free Software Foundation

+1

## 2. Điều chỉnh quyết định U-01

Tôi đề xuất chưa chuyển sang vodozemac và cũng chưa chốt libsignal ngay.

Thay vào đó, yêu cầu OpenCode:

1. Đánh giá lại AGPL-3.0 của libsignal trong mô hình KONNEX hoàn toàn mã nguồn mở.
2. Xác minh vấn đề phân phối Android trên Google Play và iOS App Store.
3. Kiểm tra giấy phép của toàn bộ dependency.
4. So sánh libsignal và vodozemac trên cùng tiêu chí bảo mật.
5. Đưa ra khuyến nghị cuối cùng có bằng chứng.

Mục tiêu là chọn giải pháp bảo mật tốt nhất, không phải chọn thư viện dễ cài nhất.

## 3. Các nguyên tắc cần ghi vào dự án

Tôi muốn những nguyên tắc này trở thành quy định lâu dài:

| Nguyên tắc | Quyết định |
| --- | --- |
| Product license | Open Source |
| Pricing | Free |
| Advertising | Chưa triển khai |
| Source code | Public |
| Backend | Public |
| Self-hosted | Có |
| E2EE | Bắt buộc theo thiết kế bảo mật |
| User data | Người dùng kiểm soát |
| Future monetization | Có thể xem xét, không ảnh hưởng quyền sử dụng miễn phí |

Hiện tại chưa có quảng cáo thì khai báo ứng dụng không chứa quảng cáo. Nếu sau này tích hợp AdMob, bạn phải cập nhật khai báo tương ứng và đáp ứng chính sách quảng cáo, quyền riêng tư của Google Play.

## 5. Bước tiếp theo tôi đề xuất

Tạo một phase nhỏ trước khi quay lại Phase 3.1:

Phase 3.0.2 – Open Source Licensing & Protocol Finalization

Mục tiêu:

- Chốt giấy phép toàn bộ KONNEX.
- Chốt chiến lược libsignal hoặc giải pháp thay thế.
- Xác nhận khả năng phân phối trên Google Play.
- Xây dựng LICENSE, NOTICE và THIRD\_PARTY\_LICENSES.
- Hoàn thiện dependency licensing matrix.
- Giữ nguyên code E2EE chưa triển khai.

Sau đó mới quyết định có đủ điều kiện bắt đầu Phase 3.1 hay không.

Tôi nghĩ đây là hướng rất phù hợp: xây KONNEX thành một ứng dụng nhắn tin mã nguồn mở thực sự, miễn phí cho mọi người, thay vì ngay từ đầu phải tối ưu nó như một sản phẩm kinh doanh.


* Done
This phase is research- and documentation-heavy. I'll start with the repository audit and the official-source research in parallel.

## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

Phase 3.1 may begin once the owner approves three items (below). 3.0.1 was blocked by an unanswered *product* question; the direction now answers it — free, fully open source, self-hostable, no paid tier — which makes the licence determinate instead of ambiguous.

| Cross-platform architecture | **APPROVED (design)** |

## What the evidence changed

- **libsignal is still AGPL-3.0-only with no §7 permission.** Three downstream requests (#677, #684 *acknowledged*, #691) are open with no grant; the 2016 App Store permission covered `libsignal-protocol-c`, not the Rust library. The AAR's POM declares AGPL 3.0 and weighs **≈122.6 MB**. Recommendation: **KEEP** — the licence conflict is zero for Play, F-Droid, direct APK and self-built iOS; only the App Store needs a third party's permission, and deferring iOS avoids it entirely.
- **OpenMLS is now audited** — SRLabs, report v1.2 (March 2026), 8 issues with 1 High, remediated in 0.8.1/0.7.3; a later High was fixed in 0.9.1. That closes 3.0.1's open audit question and is why U-02 moved to APPROVED WITH CONDITIONS.
- **Correction to 3.0.1 and `CRYPTO_LIBRARY_EVALUATION.md`:** vodozemac *was* audited (Least Authority, final report March 2022). It is Apache-2.0, current at **0.11.2**, and four advisories have landed since — including a zeroization fix in 0.11.2 and Matrix's own confirmation that Olm 3DH doesn't reject all-zero X25519 outputs.
- **Post-quantum, stated exactly:** PQXDH gives **PQ key establishment only** — Signal's own spec says authentication still needs discrete-log hardness. PQ forward secrecy and PCS require SPQR/Triple Ratchet (libsignal ≥ 0.100). **No PQ authentication exists in any deployed messenger.** Bundle 2 removes every PQ claim.
- **Two new compliance defects found in the build config:** `KonnexSdk.TARGET = 35` (Play requires **API 36** for new apps and updates since 31 Aug 2026 — the app cannot be submitted at all today), and `KonnexAndroid.kt` strips `/META-INF/LICENSE*` and `/META-INF/NOTICE*` from every APK, which conflicts with Apache-2.0 §4(d).
- **The dependency graph is clean:** 268 unique resolved coordinates, **zero AGPL/GPL/LGPL** — so KONNEX is free to choose any licence today, and the copyleft question is created entirely by adding libsignal.

## Owner decisions required

- **C-1** Product licence: AGPL-3.0-only + CC-BY-4.0 (recommended) or Apache-2.0 for Bundle 2 — blocks Phase 3.1 implementation.
- **C-2** iOS channel: App Store (needs a Signal §7 grant) / TestFlight + direct / **defer iOS** (recommended).
- **C-3** Approve raising `compileSdk`/`targetSdk` to 36 and re-running the instrumentation matrix — blocks any Play upload.
- C-4 DCO-only contribution policy, C-5 CoC + security contact, C-6 trademark policy — not Phase 3.1 blockers.

## Verified vs not verified

**Verified:** every licence claim above against upstream `LICENSE` files, POMs, release tags and advisory feeds; the live Android dependency graph via `:app:dependencies` (BUILD SUCCESSFUL, 1m 13s); current Play/Apple/F-Droid requirements; the AGPL/GPL/Apache/MPL compatibility rules from FSF, Apache and Mozilla sources.

**Not verified, and marked as such in the documents:** all native packaging for OpenMLS and vodozemac (no artifact exists, no build attempted — deliberately), libsignal's D8/Java-21 consumption, any non-Android platform, the server module's dependency report, vodozemac's Rust crate-tree licences, and any store approval. No legal approval is claimed for any option.

The nine files are `KONNEX_OPEN_SOURCE_LICENSE_STRATEGY_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `E2EE_LIBSIGNAL_REASSESSMENT_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `E2EE_VODOZEMAC_EVALUATION_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `E2EE_POST_QUANTUM_STRATEGY_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `E2EE_OPENMLS_FINAL_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `KONNEX_THIRD_PARTY_LICENSE_AUDIT_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `KONNEX_APP_STORE_DISTRIBUTION_REVIEW_PHASE3_0_2.md`, `KONNEX_OPEN_SOURCE_GOVERNANCE_PHASE3_0_2.md` and `E2EE_PHASE3_0_2_REPORT.md`. The only edit to an existing document is a forward-pointer note in `E2EE_LIBRARY_LICENSE_REVIEW_PHASE3_0_1.md` and one in `E2EE_PHASE3_0_1_REPORT.md`; both preserve the original conclusions untouched.

## 2. Ba phát hiện quan trọng

### A. OpenMLS đã có security audit

Đây là tin tốt.

OpenMLS đã có báo cáo kiểm toán SRLabs và các vấn đề được khắc phục qua những phiên bản tiếp theo.

Điều này giúp giảm một blocker quan trọng của Phase 3.0.1.

Tôi đồng ý tiếp tục giữ OpenMLS làm ứng viên chính cho Group E2EE, nhưng vẫn phải kiểm tra native integration thực tế trước khi triển khai production.

### B. libsignal vẫn là lựa chọn chính đáng cân nhắc

Với định hướng KONNEX:

- Open Source.
- Miễn phí.
- Self-hosted.
- Không quảng cáo hiện tại.

Tôi đồng ý với đề xuất giữ libsignal cho Android-first.

Tuy nhiên, cần phân biệt:

- AGPL không cấm phân phối miễn phí.
- AGPL không cấm kiếm tiền.
- AGPL yêu cầu tuân thủ nghĩa vụ tương ứng khi phân phối hoặc cung cấp dịch vụ mạng.
- Apple App Store có vấn đề riêng cần được xác minh pháp lý.

Không nên vì chưa giải quyết được iOS App Store mà loại bỏ libsignal khỏi toàn bộ kiến trúc.

### C. Post-quantum phải được diễn đạt chính xác

Đây là điểm tôi đặc biệt muốn giữ.

| Bảo đảm | PQXDH |
| --- | --- |
| Post-quantum key establishment | Có |
| Post-quantum authentication | Không |
| Post-quantum forward secrecy | Không tự động |
| Post-quantum post-compromise security | Không tự động |

Không được quảng cáo KONNEX là messenger có bảo mật hậu lượng tử toàn diện chỉ vì sử dụng PQXDH.

Đây là một quyết định rất quan trọng về uy tín bảo mật sản phẩm.

## 3. Hai lỗi kỹ thuật cần xử lý

HIGH PRIORITYGoogle Play targetSdk

Hiện tại `targetSdk = 35`, trong khi báo cáo xác định yêu cầu API 36.

Cần nâng compileSdk/targetSdk lên 36 và chạy lại toàn bộ regression tests trước khi phát hành.

LICENSE COMPLIANCEApache LICENSE / NOTICE

Build đang loại bỏ `META-INF/LICENSE*` và `META-INF/NOTICE*`.

Cần kiểm tra và sửa packaging để bảo toàn các thông báo giấy phép cần thiết, đặc biệt theo Apache-2.0 §4(d).

Tôi đề xuất xử lý hai vấn đề này bằng một phase compliance riêng hoặc một phần được phê duyệt rõ ràng trong phase tiếp theo. Không nên âm thầm thay đổi build configuration trong lúc triển khai E2EE.

## 4. Tôi đề xuất chốt ba quyết định

C-1. Giấy phép KONNEX

agpl AGPL-3.0-only + CC-BY-4.0 cho tài liệu (đề xuất)

Apache-2.0 cho source code + CC-BY-4.0 cho tài liệu

Chưa chốt, cần phân tích thêm

AGPL phù hợp nếu muốn các phiên bản server được sửa đổi và cung cấp qua mạng cũng phải đáp ứng nghĩa vụ công khai mã nguồn tương ứng.

C-2. Phân phối iOS

defer Tạm hoãn iOS App Store, ưu tiên Android (đề xuất)

iOS qua TestFlight và phân phối trực tiếp phù hợp quy định

Yêu cầu hỗ trợ Apple App Store ngay từ đầu

C-3. Android API 36

approve Đồng ý nâng compileSdk/targetSdk lên 36 và kiểm thử lại

Để phase riêng xử lý trước khi phát hành Google Play

 Xác nhận ba quyết định

## 5\. Lộ trình tiếp theo tôi khuyên dùng

Tôi muốn bổ sung một phase nhỏ để xử lý các vấn đề phát hành trước khi bắt đầu E2EE.

1. Phase 3.0.3 — Build & Open Source Compliance
   - Android API 36.
   - LICENSE/NOTICE packaging.
   - Dependency license verification.
   - Regression tests.
2. Phase 3.1 — Device Identity & Key Management
   - Device Identity Key.
   - Device Signing Key.
   - Prekey management.
   - Device registration.
   - Device verification.
   - Device revocation integration.
3. Phase 3.2 — One-to-One E2EE
   - PQXDH.
   - Double Ratchet.
   - Encrypted envelopes.
   - Session persistence.
4. Phase 3.3 — Multi-Device
5. Phase 3.4 — Group E2EE (MLS)
6. Phase 3.5 — Attachments & Recovery
7. Phase 3.6 — Security Verification

Kết luận: Tôi ủng hộ hướng AGPL + libsignal + OpenMLS cho kiến trúc đang được đề xuất, với các điều kiện pháp lý và kỹ thuật nêu trên. Không cần đổi sang vodozemac chỉ vì nó có Apache-2.0.

Bước quan trọng nhất bây giờ là chốt C-1, C-2, C-3 để chúng ta viết prompt tiếp theo mà không phải thay đổi kiến trúc giữa chừng.



Tốt. Chúng ta sẽ đi theo hướng đã thống nhất: AGPL-3.0-only + CC-BY-4.0, giữ libsignal, ưu tiên Android, tạm hoãn iOS App Store và nâng Android API 36.

Tôi đề xuất Phase 3.0.3 là phase xử lý Build & Open Source Compliance trước khi bắt đầu E2EE production.

Có một nguyên tắc quan trọng: OpenCode được phép sửa build configuration và packaging trong phạm vi đã duyệt, nhưng không được tự ý thay đổi kiến trúc mật mã hoặc dependency.

# Phase 3.0.3 — Android Build, Open Source & Distribution Compliance

Execute a controlled Android build and open-source compliance remediation phase for KONNEX.

This phase follows:

- Phase 3.0 — E2EE Architecture Design.
- Phase 3.0.1 — E2EE Protocol Qualification.
- Phase 3.0.2 — Open Source Licensing & Protocol Finalization.

The objective is to resolve the remaining Android distribution and open-source compliance issues before Phase 3.1.

This is NOT an E2EE implementation phase.

---

# 0\. Approved Project Direction

The project owner has approved the following direction.

## Product

KONNEX is:

- Completely free.
- Fully open source.
- Publicly available.
- Community-driven.
- Privacy-first.
- Self-hostable.
- Free of advertisements during the initial release.
- Designed for Android, iOS, Windows and Linux.

There are no paid features or subscriptions.

Future advertising may be considered separately but is not part of the current release.

## Licensing direction

Approved direction:

- AGPL-3.0-only for the application and server source, subject to final legal verification.
- CC-BY-4.0 for project documentation.
- Preserve all third-party licenses and copyright notices.
- Do not claim ownership of upstream cryptographic libraries.

Important: The project owner approves this licensing direction, but this is not a substitute for qualified legal review of the actual combined distribution.

## Cryptographic direction

- Keep libsignal as the primary 1:1 E2EE candidate.
- Keep OpenMLS as the group messaging candidate.
- Keep vodozemac as a documented alternative.
- Do not implement production E2EE in this phase.
- Do not change cryptographic dependencies.
- Do not weaken post-quantum security requirements.

## Platform direction

- Android is the first priority.
- iOS App Store distribution is deferred.
- Do not attempt to obtain or assume Apple's approval.
- Preserve future iOS, Windows and Linux compatibility.
- Do not introduce Android-specific wire protocol formats.

## Build direction

The project owner has approved:

- Raising Android `compileSdk` to 36.
- Raising Android `targetSdk` to 36.
- Running the complete regression and instrumentation matrix.
- Fixing the existing LICENSE/NOTICE packaging issue.

# 3\. LICENSE and NOTICE Packaging Remediation

Phase 3.0.2 identified that the current Android build strips:

- `META-INF/LICENSE*`
- `META-INF/NOTICE*`

This may conflict with third-party license obligations, particularly Apache-2.0 section 4(d).

Inspect the actual resolved dependency graph and packaged artifacts.

Requirements:

1. Preserve required upstream license notices.
2. Preserve required copyright notices.
3. Preserve Apache NOTICE content where applicable.
4. Avoid silently deleting license metadata.
5. Avoid duplicating or corrupting notices.
6. Ensure license files are accessible in the distributed application.
7. Ensure R8 does not accidentally remove required application resources.
8. Preserve third-party attribution.

Do not assume every license requires identical notice handling.

Determine the correct obligations for:

- Apache-2.0.
- MIT.
- BSD.
- MPL-2.0.
- AGPL-3.0.
- Other licenses found in the actual dependency graph.

Create a consolidated third-party notices resource or another maintainable mechanism if appropriate.

Provide an in-app Open Source Licenses screen if the existing architecture supports it without a major redesign.

If implementing that screen would introduce unrelated UI work, document the exact requirement and defer it.

Do not fabricate license texts.

Use exact upstream license texts.

Preserve copyright attribution.

## 2. Những gì cần kiểm tra khi OpenCode hoàn thành

Tôi muốn bạn gửi lại `KONNEX_PHASE3_0_3_REPORT.md`.

Đặc biệt, tôi sẽ kiểm tra 5 điểm:

1. API 36 thực sự build và chạy được, không chỉ thay số trong Gradle.
2. Các file LICENSE/NOTICE được đóng gói đúng.
3. Không có dependency mới với giấy phép không tương thích.
4. Backup KONNXBK2 cũ vẫn restore bình thường.
5. U-14b vẫn hoạt động sau migration API 36.

## Changes — 3 files, 48 insertions, 5 deletions

- `KonnexAndroid.kt`: `compileSdk`/`targetSdk` **35 → 36** (`minSdk` stays 26); removed the `META-INF/LICENSE*`/`NOTICE*` stripping.
- `SettingsScreen.kt` + `KonnexRoot.kt`: an **Open source licences** row and destination.
- New: `LICENSE` (AGPL-3.0-only, byte-exact GNU text), `NOTICE`, `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md`, five hash-recorded upstream licence texts in `assets/licenses/`, `OpenSourceLicensesScreen.kt`.

No manifest, permission, ProGuard, dependency version, schema, backup or authentication change. AGP, Gradle, Kotlin and Compose are all unchanged.

## Test evidence (all actually executed)

- **Builds:** debug, release, R8-minified release (`mapping.txt`, 42 MB), AAB (21.0 MB) — four BUILD SUCCESSFUL.
- **JVM:** 247 tests, 36 suites, **0 failures, 0 skipped**.
- **Server (re-run with `--rerun-tasks`):** 46 tests, 8 suites, **0 failures** — `DeviceAuthorizationTest` 11/11 confirms U-14b is intact.
- **Instrumentation:** API 28 **OK (105)**, API 35 **OK (105)**, **API 36 OK (105)** — plus the kill run (`Process crashed.`, by design) and recovery run (**OK (1)**) on each. **321 executions, 0 failures, 27 assumption skips** (`DeviceSyncTest` needs a second device; counted as skips, not passes).
- **16 KB pages:** `zipalign -c -P 16` → *Verification successful*, all 12 native entries.

## Three findings that change the picture

1. **The packaging defect was latent, not active.** Building without the exclusions produced **zero** `META-INF/LICENSE*`/`NOTICE*` entries — nothing was being dropped. The rule was still wrong and is gone; the obligation is now met deliberately via consolidated exact-text assets, present in both the debug **and** R8 APKs.
2. **API 36 is unsupported on the pinned toolchain.** Google requires AGP ≥ 8.9.1 (→ Gradle ≥ 8.11.1). AGP 8.7.3 builds and packages correctly but prints that it was only tested to 35. I did **not** suppress the warning or bump the toolchain unilaterally — Condition **A36-1**.
3. **The app requests capabilities it doesn't have.** The `calling` module merges `RECORD_AUDIO`, `CAMERA`, `MANAGE_OWN_CALLS` and `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT` into the release manifest for a feature that does not exist — a likely Play review failure. Removing them is a behaviour change, so it is Condition **P-5**, not a silent edit.

## Blocking Play (owner's side)

**P-1** upload keystore + Play App Signing (release currently signs with the *debug* key), **P-2** privacy policy, **P-3** Data safety form, **P-4** account deletion (server endpoint + in-app + web link), **P-5** the permission cleanup. Also **L-1** the legal review of the combined AGPL distribution, and **N-1** ARM64 testing — only x86/x86_64 emulators ran, so the ARM ABIs that ship to phones are untested.

**Not verified:** ARM devices, API 26/27, physical devices, edge-to-edge appearance on API 36 (no visual pass was performed), Play approval, and any packaging for libsignal/vodozemac/OpenMLS (none exists — forbidden this phase).

Deliverables: `KONNEX_ANDROID_API36_MIGRATION_PHASE3_0_3.md`, `KONNEX_OPEN_SOURCE_NOTICES_PHASE3_0_3.md`, `KONNEX_DEPENDENCY_LICENSE_COMPLIANCE_PHASE3_0_3.md`, `KONNEX_ANDROID_NATIVE_PACKAGING_PHASE3_0_3.md`, `KONNEX_GOOGLE_PLAY_READINESS_PHASE3_0_3.md`, `KONNEX_PHASE3_0_3_TEST_REPORT.md`, `KONNEX_PHASE3_0_3_REPORT.md`. `HEAD` is still `1d1b98d`; the tree is 47 modified + 71 untracked, with all earlier work preserved.

## 2. Bốn vấn đề cần lưu ý

### A. AGP 8.7.3 chưa chính thức hỗ trợ API 36

Đây là vấn đề tôi muốn xử lý, nhưng không cần dừng Phase 3.1.

Đề xuất:

- Giữ nguyên toolchain hiện tại trong Phase 3.1.
- Không tự ý nâng Gradle hoặc Kotlin.
- Tạo một nhiệm vụ nâng cấp AGP lên phiên bản tương thích API 36.
- Chạy lại toàn bộ build và regression sau khi nâng.

Không nên thay đổi nhiều thành phần toolchain cùng lúc.

### B. Permission gọi điện chưa sử dụng

Ưu tiên cao trước Google PlayManifest hiện có:

- `RECORD_AUDIO`
- `CAMERA`
- `MANAGE_OWN_CALLS`
- `USE_FULL_SCREEN_INTENT`

Trong khi tính năng gọi điện chưa tồn tại.

Tôi đồng ý với OpenCode: không được âm thầm xóa chúng trong phase vừa rồi.

Nhưng cần tạo một task riêng để xác định module nào đưa các permission này vào và loại bỏ khi không còn cần thiết.

### C. ARM64 chưa được kiểm chứng

Đây là vấn đề đáng quan tâm vì điện thoại Android thực tế chủ yếu sử dụng ARM64.

Cần kiểm tra:

- `arm64-v8a`.
- Native library loading.
- Android Keystore.
- Backup/restore.
- WebSocket.
- Device authorization.

Đặc biệt, khi tích hợp libsignal, phải kiểm tra ARM64 thực tế chứ không chỉ emulator x86\_64.

### D. Google Play chưa sẵn sàng

Các công việc P-1 đến P-5 vẫn còn:

| ID | Công việc |
| --- | --- |
| P-1 | Upload keystore và Play App Signing |
| P-2 | Privacy Policy |
| P-3 | Data Safety |
| P-4 | Account deletion |
| P-5 | Xử lý permission không cần thiết |
| L-1 | Legal review AGPL |
| N-1 | Kiểm tra ARM64 |

Nhưng lưu ý: chúng không phải tất cả đều là blocker của E2EE. Không cần trì hoãn phát triển mã hóa đầu cuối chỉ vì chưa chuẩn bị xong hồ sơ Google Play.

## 3. Tôi đề xuất bắt đầu Phase 3.1

Đây là giai đoạn bắt đầu xây dựng nền móng bảo mật cho từng thiết bị KONNEX.

technology circuit board key icon. Cyber data security and privacy concept

### Kiến trúc tôi muốn giữ

Một nguyên tắc cực kỳ quan trọng:

Server không bao giờ được nắm giữ private key của người dùng.

Android Device A

Device Identity + Signing + Prekeys

Private keys stay local

Public keys · Signatures · Registration

KONNEX Server

Public Key Directory · Device Registry · Revocation

No user private keys

Android Device B

Verify Device Identity · Fetch Public Prekeys

Private keys stay local

Đây là mô hình khái quát. Protocol và trust model cụ thể phải được xác định trong thiết kế Phase 3.1, không được tự phát minh.

## 4. Prompt tiếp theo: Phase 3.1

Tôi đề xuất cho OpenCode triển khai Phase 3.1 theo hướng chia thành các phần nhỏ, có kiểm thử và migration rõ ràng.

Writing

# Phase 3.1 — Device Identity & Key Management

Implement the foundational device identity and cryptographic key management architecture for KONNEX.

This phase follows the completed Phase 3.0, 3.0.1, 3.0.2 and 3.0.3.

This is the first production implementation phase of the E2EE architecture.

The goal is to establish cryptographically authenticated device identities, public-key registration, prekey management, device verification and secure local private-key storage.

Do not implement encrypted messaging, Double Ratchet or MLS in this phase.

---

# 0\. Approved Project Direction

KONNEX is:

- Completely free.
- Fully open source.
- AGPL-3.0-only licensing direction.
- CC-BY-4.0 documentation.
- Privacy-first.
- Self-hostable.
- Android-first.
- Cross-platform by architecture.
- No advertising in the initial release.

Cryptographic direction:

- libsignal remains the primary 1:1 E2EE implementation candidate.
- OpenMLS remains the group messaging candidate.
- vodozemac remains a documented alternative.
- No custom cryptographic protocol.
- No weakening of cryptographic security.

Platform direction:

- Android is the initial implementation target.
- Preserve future iOS, Windows and Linux compatibility.
- Do not introduce Android-specific wire formats.

---

# 1\. Mandatory Initial Audit

Before modifying code, inspect the actual current repository.

Read all Phase 3.0 through Phase 3.0.3 documents.

Inspect:

- Existing account authentication.
- Device registry.
- U-14b device-bound authorization.
- Device revocation.
- Server database schema.
- Existing migrations.
- Android Keystore implementation.
- Tink integration.
- Existing cryptographic utilities.
- Backup and restore.
- Application startup.
- Device synchronization.
- API specifications.
- Existing network request authentication.

Verify the actual code rather than relying on previous reports.

Record:

- Git HEAD.
- Modified files.
- Untracked files.
- Current database schema.
- Existing device authorization behavior.

Preserve all uncommitted changes.

Do not reset, clean, stash or overwrite existing work.

---

# 2\. Protocol and Library Qualification

Use the exact approved protocol and library decisions from Phase 3.0.2.

Before implementation, verify:

- Exact libsignal version.
- Actual Android artifacts.
- Rust/native ABI support.
- License.
- Java/Kotlin API surface.
- Existing cryptographic primitive support.
- Serialization and persistence requirements.

Do not invent APIs.

Do not assume an upstream API exists without inspecting its actual source or artifact.

If the selected library cannot support a required operation, stop and report the blocker.

Do not silently replace libsignal with vodozemac.

Do not implement cryptographic primitives manually.

---

# 3\. Device Identity Architecture

Define and implement a device identity model.

Every device must have an independently identifiable cryptographic identity.

The design must distinguish:

- Account identity.
- Device identity.
- Device signing identity.
- Session credentials.
- Prekey material.

Do not reuse authentication bearer tokens as cryptographic identity keys.

Do not reuse one key for unrelated cryptographic purposes unless the selected protocol explicitly specifies that reuse.

Define:

- Device identity lifecycle.
- Key creation.
- Registration.
- Verification.
- Rotation.
- Revocation.
- Re-registration.
- Device replacement.

Each lifecycle transition must be authenticated and auditable.

---

# 4\. Secure Local Key Storage

Implement secure private-key storage for Android.

Requirements:

- Android Keystore integration.
- Hardware-backed protection where available.
- Secure fallback when hardware-backed storage is unavailable.
- Clear separation of private and public key material.
- No plaintext private keys in ordinary application preferences.
- No private keys in logs.
- No private keys in crash reports.
- No private keys in analytics.
- No private keys sent to the server.
- No private keys included in ordinary backups.

Document the exact threat model for each storage mode.

Do not claim hardware-backed security unless verified using actual platform APIs.

Define behavior when:

- Keystore keys become unavailable.
- A device is restored from backup.
- The application is reinstalled.
- The device is upgraded.
- The device is locked.
- A user removes application data.

Recovery must not silently recreate an identity that the server treats as the old trusted identity.

---

# 5\. Device Signing Identity

Implement device signing identity according to the selected cryptographic protocol.

Requirements:

- Unique device signing identity.
- Server-verifiable public key registration.
- Cryptographic proof of key possession.
- Binding between device identity and account.
- Binding between device identity and device registry.
- Protection against unauthorized identity replacement.
- Explicit key rotation semantics.

Define how public keys are authenticated.

Do not treat a public key uploaded over an authenticated HTTP session as automatically trustworthy.

The server must verify the required proof of possession and authorization.

---

# 6\. Signed Prekeys and One-Time Prekeys

Implement prekey lifecycle only using the selected qualified cryptographic implementation.

Requirements:

- Signed prekey generation.
- Signed prekey registration.
- Signature verification.
- One-time prekey generation.
- Atomic one-time prekey consumption.
- Prekey replenishment.
- Expiration.
- Rotation.
- Revocation.
- Concurrent request safety.

Ensure that:

- One-time prekeys cannot be consumed twice.
- Invalid signatures are rejected.
- Unauthorized devices cannot publish prekeys for another device.
- Revoked devices cannot publish or retrieve active prekeys.
- Private prekeys remain local.

Do not invent custom prekey formats.

Follow the selected protocol specification and library APIs.

---

# 7\. Server Key Directory

Implement a public-key directory associated with the existing device registry.

Requirements:

- Account-to-device association.
- Device identity public key.
- Device signing public key.
- Signed prekey.
- One-time prekey inventory.
- Key version.
- Registration timestamp.
- Revocation state.

The directory must not store user private keys.

All registration and update operations must require authenticated device authorization.

The server must enforce the existing U-14b device-bound authorization model.

Revocation must immediately prevent further authorized key-directory operations.

Avoid leaking unnecessary account information through public lookup endpoints.

Define authorization for:

- Own-device key registration.
- Contact key lookup.
- Multi-device key lookup.
- Prekey retrieval.
- Prekey consumption.
- Device revocation.
- Device key rotation.

Do not expose private device information through unauthenticated endpoints.

---

# 8\. Device Verification and Trust

Implement a device verification model.

Distinguish:

- Registered.
- Unverified.
- Verified.
- Revoked.
- Expired or replaced.

A device must not become verified merely because the server registered it.

Define the trust transition and the cryptographic evidence required.

Investigate how the selected protocol supports device identity verification.

If safety numbers, fingerprints or equivalent verification mechanisms are supported, use the established implementation.

Do not invent a new verification protocol.

Provide a user-visible representation of device verification state.

Avoid claiming that an unverified device is trusted.

---

# 9\. Device Revocation Integration

Preserve and extend the U-14b implementation.

Requirements:

- Revocation invalidates device key-directory access.
- Revocation invalidates prekey publication.
- Revocation invalidates prekey retrieval.
- Revocation invalidates device session credentials.
- Existing sockets follow the established revocation behavior.
- Other devices belonging to the same account continue operating.
- Re-registration requires a new authenticated identity lifecycle.

Add regression tests for concurrent operations and revoked-device access.

Do not weaken the existing authorization checks.

---

# 10\. Database and Migration Safety

Inspect the existing schema before designing migrations.

Requirements:

- Forward-only migration.
- No destructive migration.
- Existing accounts remain usable.
- Existing devices remain usable.
- Existing authentication sessions remain compatible where safe.
- Revoked devices cannot regain authorization.
- Migration rollback strategy is documented.

Do not modify KONNXBK2 format.

Do not add E2EE private keys to existing backups.

Public key metadata must be treated separately from private key material.

---

# 11\. Backup and Recovery

Establish explicit identity recovery semantics.

Requirements:

- Private device identity keys are not silently copied into ordinary backups.
- Restoring an ordinary backup must not restore device trust.
- A restored installation must perform a new device registration or an explicitly designed recovery protocol.
- Existing backup compatibility remains intact.
- Lost device identity behavior must be documented.
- Device revocation and recovery must not create an identity impersonation path.

Do not implement encrypted key backup unless a separately approved recovery design exists.

---

# 12\. Cross-Platform Protocol Design

Even though implementation is Android-first, the protocol must remain platform-neutral.

Define:

- Canonical serialization.
- Key identifiers.
- Public-key encoding.
- Signature encoding.
- Version negotiation.
- Error semantics.
- Registration message formats.
- Key rotation messages.
- Revocation semantics.

Avoid Kotlin-specific serialization formats in the shared protocol.

Create interoperability test vectors where the selected protocol supports them.

Do not implement iOS or Desktop code in this phase.

---

# 13\. Testing Requirements

Add comprehensive tests.

## Cryptographic tests

- Key generation.
- Public-key serialization.
- Signature verification.
- Invalid signature rejection.
- Proof-of-possession verification.
- Prekey lifecycle.
- Key rotation.
- Identity replacement rejection.

## Authorization tests

- Two devices on one account.
- Device A cannot register keys for device B.
- Revoked device cannot publish prekeys.
- Revoked device cannot retrieve prekeys.
- Valid device remains operational after another device is revoked.
- Re-registration cannot reuse revoked credentials.

## Storage tests

- Keystore key creation.
- Key retrieval.
- Key invalidation.
- Backup exclusion.
- Application data deletion.
- Reinstallation behavior.

## Migration tests

- Existing database migration.
- Existing account compatibility.
- Existing device compatibility.
- Existing backup restore.

Run all relevant existing tests.

Do not count assumption skips as passes.

Do not claim hardware-backed Keystore behavior without actual device evidence.

---

# 14\. Explicitly Forbidden

Do not implement:

- PQXDH messaging sessions.
- Double Ratchet.
- Encrypted message envelopes.
- Message encryption or decryption.
- MLS.
- Group encryption.
- E2EE attachment encryption.
- E2EE key backup.
- History sharing.
- New advertising SDKs.
- Analytics SDKs.

Do not invent cryptographic primitives.

Do not modify unrelated modules.

Do not change dependency versions without approval.

If a required implementation depends on an unqualified native artifact, stop and report the blocker.

---

# 15\. Required Deliverables

Create:

1. `E2EE_DEVICE_IDENTITY_PHASE3_1.md`
2. `E2EE_KEY_STORAGE_PHASE3_1.md`
3. `E2EE_PREKEY_MANAGEMENT_PHASE3_1.md`
4. `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_PHASE3_1.md`
5. `E2EE_KEY_DIRECTORY_PHASE3_1.md`
6. `E2EE_DEVICE_RECOVERY_PHASE3_1.md`
7. `E2EE_PHASE3_1_TEST_REPORT.md`
8. `E2EE_PHASE3_1_REPORT.md`

Document every migration and every API change.

Preserve historical decisions.

---

# 16\. Git Safety

- Do not commit.
- Do not push.
- Do not change branches.
- Do not reset.
- Do not clean.
- Do not stash.
- Preserve all pre-existing uncommitted changes.
- Report exact files changed.
- Use disposable emulators.
- Do not use physical phones.

---

# 17\. Final Acceptance Gate

Evaluate:

- Cryptographic identity correctness.
- Private-key isolation.
- Device-bound authorization.
- Key-directory authorization.
- Prekey lifecycle correctness.
- Device verification.
- Revocation.
- Recovery behavior.
- Database migration safety.
- Backup compatibility.
- Android storage behavior.
- Cross-platform protocol compatibility.

Use:

- PASS.
- PASS WITH CONDITIONS.
- BLOCKED.
- NOT VERIFIED.

Phase 3.1 is complete only when the implemented device identity and key management behavior is supported by actual test evidence.

End with one of:

- APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.2.
- APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS.
- NOT APPROVED FOR PHASE 3.2.

Begin with repository audit.

Implement only the approved scope.

Do not implement production messaging encryption.

## 5. Một lưu ý trước khi chạy

Có một điểm tôi muốn nhấn mạnh: Phase 3.1 không nên cố hoàn thành tất cả nếu libsignal chưa có native artifact tương thích và được kiểm chứng trên ARM64.

Nếu OpenCode phát hiện thiếu API hoặc không thể tích hợp đúng, hãy yêu cầu nó dừng ở phạm vi đó, báo cáo blocker và không tự viết thuật toán thay thế.

Mục tiêu của Phase 3.1 là xây dựng danh tính thiết bị đáng tin cậy, không phải hoàn thành càng nhiều code càng tốt.


* done

## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

Half of this phase is built and proven by executed tests. The other half — the cryptography — is **blocked**, and I did not fake it.

### What works, with evidence

**Server key directory (schema v4, additive).** Four endpoints, all gated on a device-bound session: register/rotate keys, read own keys, publish prekeys, fetch a bundle. `deviceId` is the existing server device row id, so no new identifier concept was invented.

- **60 server tests, 0 failures** (was 46; +14 new).
- `403 device_mismatch` when device A writes device B's row; `409 stale_key_version` on replay/rollback; `401` unauthenticated; `403 device_revoked` on fetch of a revoked device.
- **One-time prekeys are consumed exactly once** — the `consumed_at IS NULL` predicate sits inside the `UPDATE`, so the claim is atomic, not just a serialized SELECT. Proven by 3 parallel fetches returning 3 distinct ids.
- **Revocation extended, not rewritten.** `revokeDevice` already revoked sessions transactionally; it now also marks signed prekeys revoked and deletes one-time prekeys, so a revoked device is unusable for key exchange in the same commit. Sibling devices of the same account keep working (tested).

**Local key storage.** `DeviceKeyMaterialStore` seals an opaque blob with Android Keystore AES-GCM, binds it to the installation via AAD, writes atomically via temp+rename, lives in `noBackupFilesDir`, and has three states: `Absent`, `Present`, `Lost`. `Lost` is terminal — a vanished Keystore key never silently recreates a server-trusted identity.

- 9 JVM tests assert round-trip, tamper→`Lost`, foreign file→`Lost`, clear, and that **no private-key byte appears in the stored file**.
- 2 instrumented tests on API 35: `OK (2 tests)`.

**Measured, not claimed:** the emulator logged `insideSecureHardware=false securityLevel=0`. So no hardware-backed protection is claimed anywhere in the docs — that check needs a physical device, which the brief forbids.

### What is blocked, and why

The phase's core is "cryptographically authenticated device identities". There is no key generation, no proof-of-possession, no prekey generation, and no SAS verification, because:

**libsignal is not integrated and V-1 is still open** — it ships Java 21 class files while this project builds with AGP 8.7.3 / JDK 17 / D8. Generating an Ed25519 key with BouncyCastle instead would produce material libsignal cannot use and would invent a wire identity, which the brief forbids. So `proof_verified` defaults to `0`, **no code path sets it to 1**, and every response carries `proofVerified: false`. Registration is not trust — that is the one security property this phase can enforce without a verifier, and it does.

Two consequences stated plainly: signed-prekey **signatures are stored but not verified** server-side (no verifier exists, and adding one means a new crypto dependency plus a second signature implementation), and no UI shows a "verified" badge.

| Backup compatibility | **PASS** ONNXBK2 untouched) / **NOT VERIFIED** (restore) |
| Android storage behavior | **PASS WITH CONDITIONS** (software Keystore, emulator only) |
| Cryptographic identity correctness | **BLOCKED** |
| Device verification | **BLOCKED** |

**Condition C1 gates all of Phase 3.2:** qualify libsignal (prove D8 accepts its Java 21 classes, or approve AGP ≥ 8.9.1 + Gradle ≥ 8.11.1). C2–C7 cover proof-of-possession verification, client-side signature checks, SAS, the full instrumented matrix, hardware/ARM verification, and the AGPL legal review.

- Server: **9 suites, 60 tests**, 0 failures, 0 skipped.

- `HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; nothing committed, pushed, reset, cleaned or stashed. Tree is 131 entries (48 modified + 83 untracked) with all earlier work preserved. The API 35 emulator was shut down (`adb devices` now shows only your physical phone, untouched).

Changed: 7 server files (`KonnexStore`, new `DeviceKeyRoutes`, `Dtos`, `Validation`, `Tokens`, `ServerModule`, new `DeviceKeyDirectoryTest`), 4 client files (new `DeviceKeyMaterialStore`, `KeystoreAesGcm`, new unit test, new instrumented test). No manifest, permission, dependency version, Gradle config or backup format touched.

Eight documents are in the repo root: `E2EE_DEVICE_IDENTITY_PHASE3_1.md`, `E2EE_KEY_STORAGE_PHASE3_1.md`, `E2EE_PREKEY_MANAGEMENT_PHASE3_1.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_PHASE3_1.md`, `E2EE_KEY_DIRECTORY_PHASE3_1.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_RECOVERY_PHASE3_1.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_REPORT.md`.

* done
Starting with the environment audit and the actual libsignal artifact metadata.

Artifact retrieved (122.5 MiB, AGPLv3, depends on Kotlin stdlib 2.1.0 + coroutines 1.10.2). Now inspecting actual bytecode, ABIs and ELF alignment.

Critical finding: the Android AAR's own `classes.jar` is **Java 17 (major 61)**, not 21 — and it ships **604 MB of `.so`** including `libsignal_jni_testing.so`. Checking the real Java API jar and running the actual dexer.

D8 handles it (exit 0). R8's failure was my missing stdlib on the CLI classpath, not libsignal — re-running correctly, and reading the real API signatures.

## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

**The V-1 blocker was based on a false premise, and I disproved it with the actual artifacts.**

### The claim vs. the bytes

Phase 3.0.3 asserted libsignal "ships Java 21 class files" and that this would break D8 under AGP 8.7.3 / JDK 17. I unpacked `libsignal-android:0.86.5` and `libsignal-client:0.86.5`:

```
libsignal-android classes.jar : 6 classes,   all major 61 (Java 17)
libsignal-client  jar         : 365 classes, all major 61 (Java 17)
```

There are **no major-65 class files anywhere**. Then I ran the real tools rather than reasoning about them:

| Step | Result |
|---|---|
| D8 (build-tools 36), AAR classes | **exit 0** |
| D8, 365-class client jar | **exit 0**, 540 KB dex |
| R8, both jars + stdlib | **exit 0, 0 missing classes** |
| Probe app: debug + androidTest + **release with R8** | **BUILD SUCCESSFUL** (AGP 8.7.3, Gradle 8.9, Kotlin 2.0.21, JDK 17) |
| `zipalign -c -P 16` | **Verification successful**, all four `.so` |

My first R8 run failed — but only because my CLI classpath lacked `kotlin-stdlib`. I recorded that as a harness error, not a library defect, then fixed it.

### Real cryptography, on a device

An isolated disposable app (`.logs/p311/probe`, **not** in `settings.gradle.kts`) built against the actual AAR and ran on the API 35 emulator:

**`OK (7 tests)`** — identity keygen/serialization, signature verification, tampered-message and tampered-signature rejection, wrong-identity rejection, signed prekey round-trip with signature verification, one-time prekey, **Kyber-1024** prekey, and SAS fingerprint agreement.

One run failed first (`Tests run: 7, Failures: 1`, `FingerprintVersionMismatchException`) because my harness passed fingerprint versions 1 and 2 to the two sides; both must be 2. That failing run is in the report and is not counted as a pass.

### Three findings that change the integration plan

1. **Core library desugaring is mandatory.** AGP aborts with *"Dependency 'org.signal:libsignal-android:0.86.5' requires core library desugaring to be enabled"*. Production does **not** enable it. This is a one-line flag plus a new dependency — not a toolchain upgrade.
2. **The natives are enormous and partly junk.** The AAR carries 4 valid ABIs at **16 KB `PT_LOAD` alignment**, plus `libsignal_jni_testing.so` (~318 MB raw). `libsignal-client` also bundles desktop natives *as resources* — R8 literally copied a 125 MB Linux `.so` and Windows DLLs into its output. Un-excluded, they land in the APK: **287 MB** universal release APK measured. AAB per-ABI splits are mandatory, and ABI pruning is a product decision.
3. **Two Phase 3.1 assumptions were wrong.** There is no `org.signal.libsignal.protocol.ecc.Curve` class — verification is `ECPublicKey.verifySignature`, which is what condition C3 should use. And `KeyHelper` retains only `generateRegistrationId`; key generation is `IdentityKeyPair.generate()` / `ECKeyPair.generate()` / `KEMKeyPair.generate()`.

`NumericFingerprintGenerator` works, so SAS verification (condition P1-4) is reachable using the library's own format — no KONNEX-invented scheme.

### Gate

| Item | Status |
|---|---|
| Java/D8 compatibility | **PASS** |
| R8 compatibility | **PASS** |
| Android runtime | **PASS** API 35 (x86_64); **NOT VERIFIED** API 28/36 |
| Native ABI | **PASS** packaging + x86_64 execution; **NOT VERIFIED** ARM |
| 16 KB alignment | **PASS** (ELF headers + zipalign) |
| Crypto API availability | **PASS** 7/7 |
| Test vector correctness | **NOT VERIFIED** — upstream vectors not executed |
| Key storage integration | **PASS WITH CONDITIONS** — mapping designed, envelope unchanged |
| Dependency licensing | **PASS** (AGPLv3, matches direction) / **PARTIAL** integrity |

**Explicitly not verified, and not claimed:** ARM64/ARMv7 execution (no physical device permitted), API 28/36 probe runs, release-mode crypto assertions, `readelf` dependency closure, upstream test vectors, and any security-advisory review — I ran no advisory lookup, so "no advisories" would be a fabrication.

The emulator was shut down; your phone was never touched. Repo suites re-run: **40 suites / 268 tests** and **9 suites / 60 tests**, 0 failures.

Conditions: **T1** desugaring, **T2** approve `desugar_jdk_libs:2.1.4`, **T3** approve libsignal 0.86.5, **T4** packaging exclusions, **T5** API 28/36 + ARM runs, **D1** SHA-256 + verification metadata, **D2** advisory review, **D3** CycloneDX SBOM, **S1–S4** storage envelope work, and the carried **A36-1** (AGP ≥ 8.9.1 before Play upload, unrelated to libsignal).

Deliverables: `E2EE_LIBSIGNAL_ANDROID_RUNTIME_PHASE3_1_1.md`, `E2EE_ANDROID_TOOLCHAIN_COMPATIBILITY_PHASE3_1_1.md`, `E2EE_NATIVE_ABI_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_1_1.md`, `E2EE_CRYPTOGRAPHIC_API_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_1_1.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_KEY_STORAGE_INTEGRATION_PHASE3_1_1.md`, `E2EE_DEPENDENCY_SBOM_PHASE3_1_1.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_1_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_1_REPORT.md`.

**Next:** Phase 3.1.2 can generate real device identities and publish prekeys through the existing directory — once T1–T4 land and T5 closes the platform gaps.

## 2. Ba vấn đề cần giải quyết ngay

### A. Native library quá lớn

Đây là vấn đề đáng quan tâm nhất về mặt sản phẩm.

- AAR chứa `libsignal_jni_testing.so` khoảng 318 MB.
- Các native resource desktop có thể bị đóng gói nhầm.
- Universal APK thử nghiệm lên tới 287 MB.

Không thể để các binary thử nghiệm và thư viện desktop lọt vào ứng dụng production.

Yêu cầu OpenCode chứng minh:

- APK chỉ chứa native libraries cần thiết.
- Không chứa testing native libraries.
- Không chứa Linux `.so` hoặc Windows DLL.
- AAB tạo được ABI splits phù hợp.
- Kích thước APK thực tế được đo sau R8.

### B. Core library desugaring

Phải bật desugaring và thêm dependency đã được đề xuất.

Nhưng cần kiểm tra kỹ tương thích với toàn bộ ứng dụng, không chỉ probe app.

### C. Thiếu kiểm thử trên ARM và test vectors

Đây là hai khoảng trống không nên bỏ qua.

Emulator x86\_64 chạy thành công không đồng nghĩa ARM64 chạy thành công.

Ngoài ra, 7 test tự viết chưa thay thế được upstream cryptographic test vectors.

## 3. Phase tiếp theo tôi đề xuất

Tôi muốn chia phần việc tiếp theo thành một phase đóng các điều kiện tích hợp, thay vì đưa ngay code tạo identity vào production.

Recommended next# Phase 3.1.1.1 — libsignal Production Integration Readiness

Mục tiêu: hoàn thiện tích hợp libsignal trong ứng dụng thật, giải quyết dependency, desugaring, native packaging và kiểm thử đa API.

1. Thêm core library desugaring.
2. Pin libsignal 0.86.5 và xác minh integrity.
3. Loại bỏ native testing libraries và desktop binaries.
4. Kiểm tra kích thước APK/AAB.
5. Chạy API 28, 35, 36.
6. Kiểm tra ARM64 bằng môi trường phù hợp.
7. Chạy upstream cryptographic test vectors.
8. Tạo CycloneDX SBOM.
9. Kiểm tra toàn bộ regression tests.

## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

libsignal is now **in the real app**, builds in every variant, packages only its production native library, and executes on device. No identity is active.

### Integrity first

All three artifacts match their published checksums exactly before I touched anything:

| Artifact | SHA-256 |
|---|---|
| `libsignal-android-0.86.5.aar` | `771e9a18…330a0b93` ✓ Maven |
| `libsignal-client-0.86.5.jar` | `d300c272…ff971bd` ✓ Maven |
| `desugar_jdk_libs-2.1.4.jar` | `d7604fee…29a6d28` ✓ Google Maven |

The AAR is byte-identical to the one qualified in 3.1.1, so that evidence carries over. Versions are literal pins — no ranges.

### Integration: four files, 59 insertions

`libs.versions.toml` (2 pins + 2 aliases), `app/build.gradle.kts` (desugaring + 2 dependency lines), `KonnexAndroid.kt` (packaging exclusions), and a new 7-test smoke class.

**One integration failure, found by building:** enabling desugaring in the shared convention plugin broke every library module with *"coreLibraryDesugaring configuration contains no dependencies"*. The flag belongs only where the runtime is declared, so it moved to `:app`. That is a real constraint of the plugin architecture, and it's recorded rather than papered over.

### Packaging: 0 forbidden binaries, ~600 MB saved

Verified by listing the **built** artifacts, not by reading Gradle:

- Release APK: exactly 4 × `libsignal_jni.so`; **0** matches for `libsignal_jni_testing`, `.dylib`, `.dll`.
- Excluded with **exact filenames** rather than wildcards — a `libsignal_jni_*` pattern would also match the real library and could silently drop it later.
- Upstream `acknowledgments/libsignal*.md` preserved in APK and AAB (AGPL requires it).
- `zipalign -c -P 16` **OK** on both debug and release.

| Artifact | Size |
|---|---|
| Universal debug APK | **319.8 MB** |
| Universal release APK (R8) | **298.6 MB** |
| AAB | **71.6 MB** |
| ARM64-only / x86_64-only | ≈ **85.7 / 87.3 MB** (arithmetic on measured `.so` sizes — estimates, not separate builds) |

Per-ABI installs stay under Play's limit, but 74 MB of `libsignal_jni.so` per ABI dominates the app; pruning `x86`/`armeabi-v7a` would roughly halve the AAB and is a product decision I did **not** take unilaterally.

### Tests actually run

- **API 35 full instrumented suite: OK (114 tests)**, 538.993 s, 0 failures — including the 7 libsignal smoke tests, backup/database/Keystore groups, and `DeviceSyncTest`'s assumption skips reported as skips.
- **API 36: OK (7 tests)** for the libsignal smoke suite.
- **Root JVM: 40 suites / 268 tests / 0 failures.**
- **Server: 9 suites / 60 tests / 0 failures** — `DeviceAuthorizationTest` (11) and `DeviceKeyDirectoryTest` (14) still green, so U-14b and directory authorization are untouched.

### Honest gaps

- **Upstream test vectors: NOT VERIFIED.** Nothing published for 0.86.5 is consumable through the public API — the Rust KATs are compiled into libsignal's own test binary, and the legacy `testvectors/` target an API surface that no longer exists. My 7 tests prove the *wiring and native execution*, not protocol conformance, and I do not claim the latter.
- **API 28: NOT VERIFIED** (not booted).
- **ARM64 execution: NOT VERIFIED** — packaging verified, runtime not; a shipped `.so` is not proof it runs.
- **Release/R8-mode instrumentation: NOT run** — all device tests were on the debug variant.
- **Advisory review: NOT performed.** No OSV/GHSA lookup was made, so "no known vulnerabilities" would be a fabrication.
- **Gradle verification metadata: not committed** — hashes are recorded in the SBOM doc; wiring them into the build is a separate reviewable change.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; nothing committed, pushed, reset or stashed — 149 working-tree entries (49 modified + 100 untracked) with all prior phase work intact. Both emulators shut down; your phone was never touched.

# Phase 3.1.2 — Cryptographic Device Identity Activation

Implement production cryptographic device identities for KONNEX using the qualified libsignal 0.86.5 integration.

This phase follows:

- Phase 3.0 architecture.
- Phase 3.0.1 qualification.
- Phase 3.0.2 licensing decision.
- Phase 3.0.3 Android compliance work.
- Phase 3.1 Device Identity and Key Management foundation.
- Phase 3.1.1.1 libsignal production integration.

The objective is to activate authenticated device identities and a secure prekey lifecycle.

This is NOT the one-to-one encrypted messaging phase.

## 1\. Initial Repository Audit

Inspect all existing Phase 3 documentation and implementation.

Pay special attention to:

- DeviceKeyMaterialStore.
- KeystoreAesGcm.
- DeviceKeyRoutes.
- DeviceKeyDirectory.
- Existing schema v4.
- Existing proofVerified behavior.
- Existing device authorization.
- Existing device revocation.
- Existing prekey endpoints.
- libsignal 0.86.5 integration.
- Existing backup and recovery logic.

Verify the exact current repository state.

Preserve all previous uncommitted changes.

Do not overwrite existing implementation.

## 2\. Cryptographic Architecture

Use libsignal 0.86.5 as the approved cryptographic implementation.

Use its actual public APIs.

Before implementation, verify exact method signatures against the resolved dependency.

Do not copy obsolete APIs from older Signal versions.

Use the established Signal identity and prekey structures where appropriate.

Clearly distinguish:

- Device Identity.
- Identity signing key.
- Signed Prekey.
- One-Time Prekey.
- Kyber prekey.
- Device verification state.
- Server registration state.

Do not invent a custom cryptographic protocol.

## 3\. Device Identity Generation

Implement device identity generation.

Requirements:

- Generate identity key material using the qualified library.
- Bind identity material to the existing server device identity.
- Persist private material using DeviceKeyMaterialStore.
- Keep private keys exclusively on the device.
- Publish only public material.
- Ensure atomic initialization.
- Prevent duplicate identity creation during concurrent initialization.
- Never silently replace an existing identity.

Handle Absent, Present and Lost storage states explicitly.

Lost must require an explicit device recovery or re-registration flow.

## 4\. Signed Prekey Lifecycle

Implement:

- Signed prekey generation.
- Signing with the correct identity key.
- Public signed prekey registration.
- Signature verification on the receiving client.
- Key identifier management.
- Expiration and rotation.
- Previous signed prekey retention where required.
- Revocation handling.

The server must not blindly treat a stored signature as verified.

Document which checks occur on the client and which are enforced by the server.

## 5\. One-Time Prekeys

Implement:

- Secure generation.
- Public key publication.
- Prekey inventory.
- Low-watermark detection.
- Replenishment.
- Atomic server consumption.
- Duplicate and replay protection.
- Revocation cleanup.

Preserve the existing atomic SQL consumption semantics.

Do not expose private prekey material.

Do not reuse consumed one-time prekeys.

## 6\. Kyber Prekeys and PQXDH Readiness

Inspect the actual supported libsignal 0.86.5 APIs.

Determine the exact Kyber prekey structures required by the selected Signal protocol version.

Verify:

- Key generation.
- Public key serialization.
- Signed prekey binding.
- Signature verification.
- Expiration.
- Rotation.
- Server publication.
- Client bundle validation.

Do not claim complete PQXDH interoperability merely because Kyber key generation works.

Do not implement encrypted messaging in this phase.

## 7\. Proof of Possession

Implement a server-verifiable proof that a device controls the registered identity private key.

Requirements:

- Bind the proof to the authenticated account.
- Bind it to the registered device.
- Bind it to the identity public key.
- Bind it to a fresh server challenge.
- Prevent replay.
- Expire challenges.
- Reject mismatched devices.
- Reject revoked devices.
- Ensure proof cannot be transferred to another account.

Use standard signing and verification APIs.

Do not trust a client-supplied boolean.

The server must independently verify the proof before setting proofVerified=true.

## 8\. Device Verification

Implement an explicit device verification workflow.

Use libsignal NumericFingerprintGenerator where compatible with the established Signal identity format.

Support:

- QR-based verification.
- SAS/fingerprint comparison.
- Identity key change detection.
- Verification state persistence.
- Revocation of trust after identity replacement.
- Clear user-facing verified/unverified states.

Do not mark devices verified merely because they successfully registered a key.

A new identity key must require explicit verification.

## 9\. Server Key Directory

Audit and extend the existing directory only where required.

Requirements:

- Authenticated device ownership.
- Proof-of-possession enforcement.
- Signature validation.
- Key versioning.
- Replay protection.
- Atomic prekey consumption.
- Device revocation.
- Audit events.
- Consistent transaction boundaries.

Preserve sibling-device independence.

Do not redesign the complete server authorization system.

## 10\. DeviceKeyMaterialStore Integration

Ensure all private cryptographic material is stored through the existing protected storage abstraction.

Review:

- Identity private key.
- Signed prekey private material.
- One-time prekey private material.
- Kyber private material.
- Required library state.
- Serialization versioning.
- Encryption envelope versioning.
- Atomic writes.
- Keystore invalidation.
- Application reinstall.
- Backup exclusion.

If an envelope migration is needed, design a versioned migration.

Never silently discard an existing identity.

Never log private key material.

## 11\. Recovery and Revocation

Test:

- Normal identity initialization.
- Repeated initialization.
- Concurrent initialization.
- Keystore key invalidation.
- Corrupted encrypted storage.
- Application reinstall.
- Device revocation.
- Device re-registration.
- Stale prekey retrieval.
- Old proof replay.
- Identity replacement.

A revoked device must not be able to restore trust merely by replaying old credentials.

Recovery must not silently copy one device's private keys into another device.

## 12\. Testing

Add unit tests for:

- Identity key generation.
- Identity serialization.
- Signature verification.
- Invalid signature rejection.
- Signed prekey verification.
- One-time prekey lifecycle.
- Kyber prekey lifecycle.
- Proof challenge expiration.
- Proof replay rejection.
- Device mismatch.
- Revocation.
- Concurrent initialization.
- Storage loss.

Add server tests for:

- Valid proof.
- Invalid proof.
- Replayed proof.

## 5\. Điều tôi muốn đạt được sau Phase 3.1.2
