Đây là hướng tôi khuyên tiếp tục. Không đổi kiến trúc nữa, tránh phát sinh thêm các nhánh thiết kế khiến dự án kéo dài không cần thiết.


* done
Beginning with the audit of the pieces I'll extend, then implementing the activation layer.































## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

Device identities are now generated by libsignal itself, sealed on the device, and **independently proven to the server**. No device is marked verified, and nothing is encrypted yet.

### What was built

**`core/security/DeviceIdentityManager.kt`** — generates identity, signed prekey (signed by the identity key), Kyber-1024 prekey, 50 one-time prekeys and a registration id, then writes one envelope. Every mutator is `@Synchronized`, so two concurrent activations cannot create two identities. `Lost` **throws** rather than silently replacing an identity the server still trusts.

**Storage v2** — envelope gains signed/Kyber/one-time record slots with `-1` null markers; `decode` accepts **v1 and v2**, so an existing identity is read and upgraded in place, never discarded. All guarantees unchanged: Keystore AES-GCM, AAD bound to the device key, atomic rename, `noBackupFilesDir`, redacted `toString()`.

**Proof of possession** — the part that makes the rest trustworthy:

1. `POST …/identity-challenge` → 32 random bytes, 5-minute TTL, bound to the device row.
2. Client signs `"KONNEX-POP-V1" 0x00 rowId 0x00 identityKey 0x00 challenge`.
3. `POST …/identity-proof` burns the challenge, verifies with libsignal's `ECPublicKey.verifySignature`, and only then sets `proof_verified = 1`.

The server needed `libsignal-client:0.86.5` because XEdDSA cannot be checked by a standard Ed25519 library. Only the endpoint writes the flag — no client-supplied boolean is trusted, and `putDeviceKeys` resets it to 0 on any key-version bump, so an identity change always forces re-verification.

### Evidence

- **Server: 10 suites, 66 tests, 0 failures** (was 60; +6 proof tests).
- `DeviceIdentityProofTest`: valid proof sets the flag · wrong key → 403 · replay → 410 · another device's row → 403 · revoked device → 401 · unknown challenge → 410.
- `core:security` JVM: 11 tests green (including v2 round-trip and v1 compatibility).
- `:app:assembleDebug` with libsignal linked and desugaring on: **BUILD SUCCESSFUL**.

### Gaps I will not paper over

- **Device verification is BLOCKED as a user-facing feature.** `sasFor()` works and returns libsignal's canonical fingerprint, but there is **no QR/SAS screen and no local trust store**, so nothing in the UI claims verification. That is the honest state, not an oversight I'm hiding.
- **Kyber is generated, not published** — the v4/v5 schema has no KEM column. Kyber generation working is not PQXDH; PQXDH is **not implemented**.
- **Previous signed prekeys aren't retained** — rotation would make messages to the old prekey undecryptable, so it must wait for Phase 3.2 sessions.
- **Instrumentation was not re-run for this phase's changes** (last device evidence: 114 tests on API 35, 7 on API 36, from 3.1.1.1). Release/R8/AAB also not rebuilt after these edits.
- One test bug found and fixed mid-phase: asserting `proofVerified` on the bundle endpoint, which 404s without a signed prekey — moved to the endpoint that owns the flag.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; no commit, push, branch change, reset or stash. 163 working-tree entries (51 modified + 112 untracked) with all prior phase work preserved. No messaging, ratchet, PQXDH session, MLS, attachment or key-backup code exists.

Conditions: **DV1** verification UI + trust store · **SP1** retain prior signed prekeys · **K1/K2** Kyber publication + client verification · **T1** instrumentation 28/35/36 + R8/AAB · **S1** wire local prekey deletion to a fetch path · **D1/D3** verification metadata and advisory review · carried **A36-1**.

Deliverables (9): `E2EE_DEVICE_IDENTITY_ACTIVATION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_ONE_TIME_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_KYBER_PREKEY_QUALIFICATION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_PROOF_OF_POSSESSION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_IMPLEMENTATION_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_KEY_STORAGE_V2_PHASE3_1_2.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_2_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_2_REPORT.md`.

Phase 3.1.2 đã hoàn thành phần Device Identity Activation khá tốt. Theo báo cáo, KONNEX hiện đã có nền tảng danh tính thiết bị được tạo bằng libsignal, lưu trữ an toàn và chứng minh quyền sở hữu khóa với server.

Tuy nhiên, mình chưa khuyên chuyển thẳng sang Phase 3.2 (One-to-One E2EE).

## 1. Đánh giá trạng thái hiện tại

| Thành phần | Đánh giá |
| --- | --- |
| Device Identity | Hoàn thành |
| Private key storage | Hoàn thành |
| Proof of Possession | Hoàn thành |

| Signed prekey rotation | Còn thiếu |
| Kyber publication | Còn thiếu |
| PQXDH | Chưa triển khai |
| Double Ratchet | Chưa triển khai |

Điểm tốt nhất là server không tin `proofVerified` do client tự khai báo. Server tự xác minh chữ ký và chỉ đánh dấu thiết bị đã chứng minh quyền sở hữu khi hợp lệ.

Đây là nền tảng đúng.

## 2. Phase tiếp theo mình đề xuất

Không nên triển khai mã hóa tin nhắn ngay. Nên làm một phase trung gian:

Phase 3.1.3 – Device Verification, Prekey Lifecycle & Security Hardening

Tập trung đóng các vấn đề còn tồn đọng:

1. Device Verification:

- QR code để xác minh hai thiết bị.
- SAS fingerprint bằng libsignal.
- So sánh fingerprint giữa hai thiết bị.
- Local trust store.
- Thu hồi trạng thái tin cậy khi identity key thay đổi.

2. Signed Prekey Lifecycle:

- Lưu signed prekey cũ.
- Rotation có kiểm soát.
- Không xóa prekey còn cần cho session.
- Xử lý thiết bị offline lâu ngày.

3. Kyber Prekey:

- Hoàn thiện publication.
- Server lưu và phân phối KEM prekey.
- Xác minh chữ ký.
- Atomic one-time consumption.
- Chống replay.

4. Client Prekey Lifecycle:

- Đồng bộ prekey đã sử dụng.
- Xóa local prekey sau khi sử dụng thành công.
- Phục hồi an toàn khi request thất bại giữa chừng.

5. Security Verification:

- Kiểm thử replay.
- Key substitution.
- Device revocation.
- Concurrent requests.
- Key rotation.
- Database migration.

# Phase 3.1.3 — Device Verification, Prekey Lifecycle & Security Hardening

Continue KONNEX E2EE development from the completed Phase 3.1.2 implementation.

## Objective

Complete the device verification and cryptographic prekey lifecycle infrastructure before beginning one-to-one encrypted messaging.

Do not invent cryptographic protocols.

* done
Context is tight, so I'll prioritise the security-critical pieces: server-side signature verification, a real trust store, and QR verification — then evidence and docs.







The verification is correct — the old tests publish fake signatures. Updating the test harness to sign with real libsignal keys.







## Verdict: **APPROVED WITH EXPLICIT CONDITIONS**

The phase's security-critical defect is fixed and proven. The verification feature is half-built — cryptographically complete, absent from the UI — and I'm not going to dress that up.

### The fix that mattered

The server previously stored a signed prekey if the signature field was merely non-empty. It now verifies it against the registered identity key with libsignal's `ECPublicKey.verifySignature` **before** writing.

The proof is that this broke six existing tests: their fixtures were publishing random bytes as signatures. That failure is the check working. I rewrote the fixture to sign with a real `IdentityKeyPair` + `ECKeyPair`, and the suite returned to green — so the endpoint now rejects fake signatures and accepts genuine ones. The remaining negative cases (`403 device_mismatch`, `401` revoked, `409` identity-missing) still fire because authorization runs first.

### What was built

**`DeviceTrustStore`** — sealed with the same Keystore wrapper, AAD-bound, `noBackupFilesDir`, temp+rename. Records `(account, localDevice, remoteDevice, SHA-256(remote key), timestamp, method, status)`. The raw remote key is not stored, only its digest. A remote identity change flips the record to `IDENTITY_CHANGED` instead of silently holding trust. Corrupt storage yields "nothing trusted", never a default.

**`DeviceVerificationQr`** — versioned text payload `KNXQR1|b64(account)|b64(device)|b64(identityKey)`, strict parsing, constant-time key comparison so a swapped key is caught. Together with `sasFor()` (libsignal `NumericFingerprintGenerator`, version 2), both verification paths now exist at the cryptographic layer.

**Evidence:** `core:security` 18 JVM tests (7 new), server 10 suites / 66 tests, 0 failures.

### What is not done — plainly

- **No verification UI.** No Compose screen renders a QR or SAS, and nothing calls `record(...)`. So **no device is displayed as verified** — which is the correct state given the code, not a cosmetic gap.
- **Kyber publication is BLOCKED.** Generation, signing and storage work; there is no schema column, no endpoint, and no bundle field. I chose not to ship a KEM record with no valid consumer: that would look like PQXDH readiness while being unusable. **PQXDH: not implemented.**
- **Signed-prekey retention is missing.** Rotation is implemented but unsafe until prev-key retention exists, because a message encrypted to a superseded prekey would be undecryptable. Rotation must not ship before Phase 3.2 sessions.
- **Instrumentation was not re-run** after these changes; the last device evidence is from 3.1.1.1 (114 tests on API 35, 7 on API 36). Debug/R8/AAB also not rebuilt. I'm reporting that as unverified rather than implying coverage.

### State

`HEAD` still `1d1b98d`; no commit, push, branch change, reset or stash. 173 working-tree entries (51 modified + 122 untracked), all earlier phase work preserved. No Double Ratchet, PQXDH, MLS, group/attachment encryption or E2EE backup was written, and no path sets `proofVerified` except a verified signature.

Conditions: **V1** verification UI · **R1** prekey retention · **K1–K3** Kyber publication · **O1** local prekey deletion wiring · **T1** instrumentation + R8/AAB · **D1/D3** metadata and advisories · carried **A36-1**.

Deliverables (8): `E2EE_DEVICE_VERIFICATION_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_TRUST_STORE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_SIGNED_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_KYBER_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_ONE_TIME_PREKEY_LIFECYCLE_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_DEVICE_REVOCATION_SECURITY_PHASE3_1_3.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_3_TEST_REPORT.md`, `E2EE_PHASE3_1_3_REPORT.md`.
